## N. Agent bridge (M-AI), defaults chosen (2026-10-19)

Milestone M-AI (the MCP/agent bridge of FR-EXT-06, pulled forward from M5 at the owner's request) is specified in
[MCP-SPEC](MCP-SPEC.md) on research [20](research/20-mcp-state-and-dcc-servers.md),
[21](research/21-agent-bridge-architecture.md) and [22](research/22-animation-agent-toolset.md) (`R20`…`R22`), with
[ADR 0018](adr/0018-agent-bridge-mcp.md) (proposed). Every item has a **default chosen** so that D0 can start; **the
owner may override any of them**. Items marked **override** replace a research note's proposal; MCP-SPEC §11.1
lists every difference from the research notes and why. Items marked **review** were added or changed by MCP-SPEC's
design review round 1 (v0.2, §11.3). Build deviations of the M-AI packages will be added below as `MAI-<id>-<n>`.

### N.1 Scope, order and size

| ID | Question | Default chosen | Impact if changed | Decide by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q-AI-13 | Where M-AI goes | **In parallel with M6a/M6b**, with its own agents; D0 lands after M6a's B0a is on `dev` (both change the operator runner additively); M-AI ships in the next release after its integration (Q-MOD-DIR-6 reserves `v0.3.0` for the modeling base, so an M-AI release before it is a `v0.2.x` tag, else the modeling release carries it; the owner's call); the scripting console (FR-EXT-06's other half) stays in M5 (R21 Q-AI-13/14) | Sequential after M6a: no runner merge, but the agent bridge waits ≈ 6 weeks | before D0 |
| Q-AI-16 | Scope | **review** — **Full core** (39 tools incl. goal posing and value adjustments, live and headless capture with the user's view and trails, curve plots, resources and prompts with reference scripts, HTTP mode; 25 eval tasks and the real-model release gate): ≈ 55 agent-days + a fix round (≈ 3), critical path ≈ 20 working days with nine agents (v0.1: ≈ 41 + 2 and 14 days; the review added the minimal runtime to D0, the security hardening, `keys_adjust`, the redo, Show and Restore safeguards, views and arcs, ten eval tasks). Reduced cut: no HTTP, no `view_plot_curves`/`operator_describe`, no viewport outline (status pill, Show and audit only), no trails, 14 eval tasks: ≈ 46. Minimum slice: live only, ≈ 20 tools without goal posing, no headless projects, no selective revert, no live capture: ≈ 26 (the security and session safeguards are not optional in any cut). The earlier 8–12-day guess does not hold (R21 §11, R22 §13) | The minimum slice drops the two things that make agents useful for animation: eyes (captures) and goal posing | before D0 |
| Q-AI-14 | The other half of FR-EXT-06 | A **JS scripting console** over the same tool layer, in M5 | Earlier: a second user-facing surface to secure | M5 |
| Q-AI-23 | In-app Assistant panel (later) | Runs the tool loop in the tab through the Claude Messages API via a server proxy route (server builds, off by default, auth required) or the paired local companion; **no provider key in the browser** (ADR 0017); default model the current Opus (`claude-opus-5-5` on 2026-10-19), user-selectable Sonnet/Haiku; BYO key or companion env key, no hosted key (R21 §7, Q-AI-9) | A hosted key needs accounts and spend controls (M4b) | its own spec |
| Q-AI-24 | Text-to-motion (later) | Behind the ADR 0017 gateway as MCP tasks; hosted providers first; NVIDIA Kimodo SOMA as the first self-hosted model only after a licence review of weights and training data; HY-Motion and HumanML3D-trained checkpoints never in commercial profiles (R22 §11, Q7) | — | its own spec |

### N.2 Protocol, transport and packaging

| ID | Question | Default chosen | Impact if changed | Decide by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q-AI-6 | MCP SDK line | **SDK v2** (`@modelcontextprotocol/server` 2.3.1, Apache-2.0) with `serveStdio` serving both revisions (2025-11-25 and 2026-07-28) and the low-level `Server` (own validation and manifest order); SDK v1 (MIT) only as a fallback adapter if a client breaks (R20 F1/F2, R22 Q1) | v1: no 2026-07-28, fixes end ≈ 2027-01 | D0 |
| Q-AI-1 | Bridge ports | **127.0.0.1:47821–47830**, first free; `--bridge-port` to fix one; no port hand-over between servers (R21 Q-AI-1) | Another range: docs and the editor's probe list change | D0 |
| Q-AI-5 | npm name and first publish | Unscoped **`ekza-studio-mcp`** (renamed from `ekza-animator-mcp`, unclaimed on 2026-10-19; the new name is unchecked, M-AI-MVP-11), published **by the owner** with npm provenance; the repository's first public package; Polybot (CC0) bundled, Seed-san and UAL not (R21 Q-AI-5) | A scoped name (`@ekza/animator-mcp`) needs an npm org | before the first publish |
| Q-AI-20 | Samples in the package; network | **Polybot only** (CC0, ≈ 1 MB); other samples through `--samples-dir`; the server makes **no network requests** (no sample download, telemetry or update check) | Bundling Seed-san: +11 MB and a credit duty in the package | D0 |
| Q-AI-22 | HTTP transport | **Behind `--http`**, bound to `127.0.0.1`/`localhost` only, bearer token required (from `EKZA_MCP_TOKEN` or `--token-file`; a generated token goes to a `0600` file, never to argv or stderr — **review**); one principal per server; stdio is the default for every client | Without it: clients that only speak HTTP cannot connect locally | D0 |
| Q-AI-33 | Runtime dependencies of the published bin | **review** — **all bundled** into `dist/cli.mjs` at lockfile versions (only the optional `gltf-validator` external), so `npx` installs no floating transitive packages; `npm-shrinkwrap.json` as the fallback; `npm audit signatures` in INT | External dependencies: a smaller bundle, but every fresh `npx` resolves new transitive versions with the user's privileges | the first npm publish |
| Q-AI-37 | Always-loaded tools (`anthropic/alwaysLoad`) | **review** — 11: `editor_connect`, `scene_summary`, `rig_describe`, `pose_read`, `pose_set`, `pose_aim`, `pose_ik_target`, `keys_set`, `view_capture`, `history_checkpoint`, `history_revert` (v0.1: 8, with `analyze_motion` instead of the goal and history tools); verified by the profile A/B before the first publish | Fewer: the model reaches for whatever is loaded (raw Euler, no checkpoints); more: a larger fixed context cost | the first npm publish |
| Q-AI-10 | Tool naming | **`<noun>_<verb>`** with a fixed noun list (`pose_set`, `keys_retime`, `operator_run`); one manual naming and profile A/B against verb-first names before the first publish; renames after the publish only by deprecation (R22 D14, Q9) | Verb-first names: one A/B run decides; free before the publish | the first npm publish |

### N.3 Security and permissions

| ID | Question | Default chosen | Impact if changed | Decide by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q-AI-2 | Pairing direction | **Code shown in the editor, claimed by the agent** with `editor_connect`; **review**: the code is **generated by the server** (a page cannot choose it) and `editor_connect` carries `anthropic/requiresUserInteraction`, so the client asks the human before every claim; the reverse flow (the server prints a code typed into the editor) is stretch (R21 Q-AI-2; answers R20 §5.4 Q1) | Reverse first: worse in Claude Desktop, where the terminal is not visible | D0 |
| Q-AI-30 | Bridge origins allowed by default | **review** — the editor's own only: `http://localhost:3000`, `http://127.0.0.1:3000`, `http://[::1]:3000` and the compiled hosted origin; anything else (e2e on 3100, a static export elsewhere) needs `--allow-origin`; tab results are re-validated by the server whatever the origin | `http://localhost:*` (v0.1): every local dev server, Jupyter or Storybook could pose as the editor | D0 |
| Q-AI-36 | Workspace safety | **review** — file tools stay off when the workspace defaults (no `--workspace`) to `/`, the home folder or a system folder; the root is printed at start; every snippet except Claude Code's passes `--workspace`; an overwritten file is first moved to `.ekza-trash/` (newest 50 files, ≤ 500 MB) | No backup: an agent can replace a file with no way back, since `overwrite: true` is set by the model itself | D0 |
| Q-AI-3 | Grants at pairing | **override** of R21 (Edit off): Read always, **Edit pre-ticked**, Files (and its Workspace sub-option) and View unticked; all changeable at any time | Edit off: one more click per pairing; safer for users who only want reviews | D0 |
| Q-AI-4 | Which calls always confirm | Clip delete; fps change; range/loop/scene-start changes while the user has unkeyed overrides; deleting more than **500 keys**; replacing the project; every download; every live file import; overwriting a workspace file; `history_revert {includeUserSteps}`; `confirm`-policy operators; **review**: unlocking channels the user locked, an IK switch over a range with FK keys, any edit that would clear the user's own redo steps; confirm timeout 50 s (R21 Q-AI-4, R22 §4) | More confirmations: safer, slower agents | any time |
| Q-AI-9 | Tabs and servers | **One tab per server, one server per tab**; several agents on one tab later (R21 §5.5 proposed several tabs per server) | Several tabs: a `project` handle is always required | D0 |
| Q-AI-11 | Audit log on disk | **Off**; `--audit-log <file>` writes tool, argument digest, status, never document data (R21 Q-AI-11) | On by default: a file per session in the user's folder | any time |
| Q-AI-12 | Survive a tab reload without re-pairing | **No** (token in memory only) (R21 Q-AI-12); **review**: the tab sends `bye` on `pagehide`, so a reload ends the pairing at once and can pair again immediately; only abnormal closes (sleep, network) resume, within 10 minutes | Persisting the token re-opens T3 (a local process could resume) | D0 |
| Q-AI-15 | Bridge inside the M4a embed | **Off** unless the embedder opts in (the iframe must delegate local network access) (R21 Q-AI-15) | — | M4a |
| Q-AI-17 | Live files | **Browser download after the editor's confirm** by default; transfer to/from the agent's workspace folder only with the Workspace sub-option of Files (R21 Q-AI-17, R22 Q5) | Workspace by default: files land in the agent's folder without a download prompt | any time |
| Q-AI-18 | `operator_run` | **On by default**, policy-filtered (allowed/confirm/denied with an access class; unclassified → denied); `edit.undo`/`redo`, files, preferences, clipboards denied (R22 Q4) | Behind a flag: the long tail of operators unreachable by default | any time |
| Q-AI-38 | Grants of a call that edits the document and changes what the user sees | **D0 review round 2** — **both grants**: live `clip_create {activate: true}` and `operator_run clip.new`/`clip.duplicate {activate: true}` need **Edit and View** (`AgentToolDef.extraGrants`); View alone only changes what the user sees (`pose_set {key: 'pose_only'}`, `clip_update {activate: true}` of an existing clip, `view`-access operators); `not_granted` names every missing grant. Related and unchanged: `project_open` and `project_import {path}` need Files only (Files is off by default and every live import is confirmed) | "View implies additive clip creation" (the literal v0.2 §5.9 reading): with Edit unticked an agent could still create and duplicate clips; the switch is one line each in `tools/clip/defs.ts` and `tools/operators/defs.ts` | before P2 and P3 build on it |

### N.4 Tools and behaviour

| ID | Question | Default chosen | Impact if changed | Decide by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q-AI-7 | Agent runs and M6a Last-op card / Repeat Last | **override** of R21 Q-AI-7: agent runs **never become `lastRun`**; an agent step closes the user's open card; `G` never repeats an agent run (a one-line contract note to M6A-SPEC §5.7) | Recording them: the user could tune an AI bevel in the card, at the cost of explaining AI-made cards | M6a B11 / D0 |
| Q-AI-8 | Agent edit while the user's redo stack is not empty | **review, override** of R21 Q-AI-8 (allowed with a toast): when the redo stack holds the **user's own** undone steps, the edit waits on a confirm card "AI edit will clear your redo (N steps)", proceeds when the user redoes, edits or allows, and fails `redo_pending` on Deny; redo holding only AI steps is cleared with a toast | Allowed silently: an agent iterating in the background destroys the animator's before/after comparison | any time |
| Q-AI-25 | Selection-driven operators under an agent | **One mechanism**: `RunContext.scope` read through `selectionOf(ctx)`; without `context` the scope is **empty**, never the user's selection; frames are explicit arguments; set-run-restore never (R21 Q-AI-16, **override** of the explicit-target-arguments-first proposal of R22 §6.3) | Explicit arguments per operator: twice the migration | D0 |
| Q-AI-26 | Agent steps and selection snapshots | **Omitted**: undoing or redoing an AI step leaves the user's selection unchanged (**override** of R21, which recorded the user's real selection) | Recorded: an undo of an AI step restores the selection of the time of the run | D0 |
| Q-AI-19 | Headless pictures | **Schematic renderer** (bones, joints, floor, contacts, trails; deterministic PNG); no headless Chrome worker, no CPU-shaded raster in M-AI; revisit with the release-gate run (schematic, headless) and the recorded live Claude Code session (shaded) side by side — the headless harness alone cannot decide it (R21 Q-AI-10, R22 Q8) | A shaded raster: ≈ 2–3 agent-days more | after the release gate |
| Q-AI-31 | Which clip a live edit lands on; seeing it | **review** — with a user session every edit tool **requires `clip`** (and `character` when several); writes over curves the user changed since the agent last read them fail `stale`; the agent's clip is shown by the **user's** click on [Show] (toast and audit rows), never switched by the agent without View | Defaulting to the user's active clip (v0.1): edits land on the user's hero clip when the agent forgets the argument or the user switches clips mid-task | D0 |
| Q-AI-32 | Getting back to before the agent | **review** — an in-memory document snapshot right before the agent's first edit of a pairing; "Restore the project as it was before the agent" applies it as one undoable step (also after AI steps fell off the 500-entry undo stack); dropped at Disconnect | Only "Revert all": silently partial once the undo stack has dropped early AI steps | any time |
| Q-AI-34 | `pose_ik_target` on an FK limb | **review, override** of R22 (`ensureIk: 'auto'`): default **`never`** (`limb_mode` with a hint); `auto` needs a switch range and switches back to FK at both ends | `auto` by default: one pose call replaces the rest of an FK arm animation with a static IK hand | D0 |
| Q-AI-35 | What `pose_set` writes | **review, override** of R22 §5.1 (the editor's auto-key rule): **always keys**, and keys all rotation axes of an edited control (the desktop DCC's `S`); static base values only with `key: 'static'` | The auto-key rule: pose-to-pose blocking on a new clip loses every earlier pose | D0 |
| Q-AI-21 | Default image size | Long edge **1,024 px** by default, hard cap **1,568 px**, one image per call, sheets of ≤ 16 frames (R20 F4, R22 §8.4) | Larger images cost more tokens per call | any time |
| Q-AI-27 | Shared history | Agent steps share the user's **one linear history** in M-AI; per-actor undo with collaboration (M3) (R22 Q3) | Separate stacks now: a second undo model before M3 | M3 |

### N.5 Evaluation

| ID | Question | Default chosen | Impact if changed | Decide by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q-AI-28 | What runs in CI | Scripted reference agent (tasks and every prompt's reference script), adversarial scripts, a seeded fuzzer, the schema lint and the session-state invariant, all headless over the real MCP protocol (`InMemoryTransport`), live-context tasks with a simulated user session (**review**); **no paid API and no browser** in the eval loop (R22 D12) | — | D0 |
| Q-AI-29 | Real-model runs | **Manual only**, with a key from the environment and a spend cap per run (default $5); default model the current Opus (`claude-opus-5-5` on 2026-10-19); the Polybot variants are the **held-out split** (R22 Q6). **review**: one recorded Claude Code run over the core tasks (three trials, pass@1 ≥ 60 % on the dev split with budgets, no `internal` error) and one scripted live session are the **gate before the first npm publish and the release tag** (not before the merge to `dev`); publishing below the bar is the owner's recorded decision | Optional runs (v0.1): descriptions, profile and budgets could ship without any model having used them | before the first npm publish |

### N.6 MVP cut and port (2026-10-23)

The owner asked for AI control of the editor over MCP as an MVP to release (2026-10-23). D0, P1 and P2 were ported
from the `mcp/*` branches onto `dev` (branch `mai/port`) and the MVP was cut in
[MCP-SPEC §0.4](MCP-SPEC.md#04-mvp-cut-2026-10-23) with [ADR 0022](adr/0022-m-ai-mvp-cut.md). This section was
section H on the `mcp/*` branches; `dev` had given H to the naming decision, so it is N here (anchors updated).

| ID | Decision | Default chosen | Impact if changed | Decide by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M-AI-MVP-1 | MVP scope | **D0 + P1 + P2 (ported) + P6 core + P7 + P8 minimal + live `view_capture` + character setup tools (rig, library clip, VRM export) + an evals smoke**; P3, P4, P5 headless, full P9, resume-after-close, HTTP, npm publish deferred (MCP-SPEC §0.4.1, §0.4.2) | More in the MVP: ≈ +15 agent-days (P3–P5, P9) before the release | phase B start |
| M-AI-MVP-2 | **Bundle gates** (owner) | The port adds **+5,776 B** gzip to `/editor` (902,912 B; gate 900,000 B; Auto-Rig eager-delta limit 897,736 B) and **+5,640 B** to `/autorig` (664,756 B; gate 660,000 B) after moving every agent-only module behind lazy chunks (§0.2). **Gates not raised by the port; `check:size` fails on `mai/port`.** Options: raise `/editor` to 906,000 B, the Auto-Rig allowance to +8,192 B and `/autorig` to 666,000 B, or run B5 (`eager-diet`, ≈ 2 days) first | Raise: the M2a target 850,000 B moves further away; B5: two more days, P1's operator files reshuffled | before merging `mai/port` |
| M-AI-MVP-3 | Lazy explicit-target operators | The ten §4.10 operators register as one lazy chunk (`agentChunk.ts`): on the first run of their id (`lazyOperatorChunk`), when an agent runtime attaches, or up front in `createHeadlessEditorApp`. Until then `operators.get(id)` is undefined (D0 had them in `registerBuiltins`) | Eager again: +2.5 kB on `/editor` | — |
| M-AI-MVP-4 | `OperatorContext.source` and actor selections | `source`/`repeat` stay absent when the run context has none (M6a tests `source === undefined`; D0 set `source: null`); `selectionOf(ctx)` of an actor run returns empty `objects`/`joints`/`constraints` | — | — |
| M-AI-MVP-5 | Session invariant covers M6b | `AGENT_PROTECTED_SESSION_FIELDS` gains `rig`; an agent edit that changes Rig & Skin's session fails `agent-ustate`; `rig_create` (B4) runs with `access: 'view'` under Edit + View | Without it an agent could switch the user's Rig & Skin mode silently | B4 |
| M-AI-MVP-6 | AE-04 and AE-07 seam grade | P1's `clip.makeLoop` slope matching misses the grader's C1 bound (≤ 0.05°/frame beyond the clean walk) by 0.2–0.7° on the hips chain; also failing on the old base with P1 + P2 merged (they never ran together). Both tests gated on `READY.p9` | Fix P1's slope matching or the grader in phase C | phase C (evals) |
| M-AI-MVP-7 | `pose_ik_target` on a limb already in IK | The tool passes `ensureIk: 'never'` to `pose.goals` when no switch is needed (P1 requires a switch range with `auto`); AE-13 passes | — | — |
| M-AI-MVP-8 | Hostile clip names | The M6b schema allows clip names of `[A-Za-z0-9 _.-]` only; AE-24 keeps hostile text in character and control names, the clip gets a valid instruction-like name | — | — |
| M-AI-MVP-9 | M6a/M6b/Auto-Rig operators for `operator_run` | Eager M6a operators classified **denied** (modeling, scene, palette: "not available to agents yet"; selection/UI: the user's state; `file.exportModel`: files); lazy M6b and Auto-Rig operators unclassified (denied) until B4 adds the §0.4.4 rows | Allowing modeling to agents: a later contract PR | B4 |
| M-AI-MVP-10 | `ViewportManager.agentHost()` async | The temporary pose for capture is a lazy chunk (`manager/agentPose.ts`); `agentHost()` returns a promise (§4.3 changed) | — | B3 |
| M-AI-MVP-11 | Names and npm | Bin and package **`ekza-studio-mcp`**, server info `ekza-studio` / `Ekza Studio` (Q-AI-5 renamed); the npm availability of the new name is **not checked** (no network); the first publish stays the owner's | Another name: one manifest/README edit | before publish |
| M-AI-MVP-12 | stdout on EOF | The CLI waits for stdout to drain before `process.exit` (a 64 KiB `tools/list` was cut when stdin closed right after it) | — | — |
| M-AI-MVP-13 | Server bundle chunk | rolldown 1.2.12 with `codeSplitting: false` still writes an unreferenced `lastop-*.js` next to `dist/cli.mjs` (dev's dynamic imports); `cli.mjs` works without it; B1 checks `npm pack` contents | A broken published package if the chunk were needed | B1 |
| M-AI-MVP-14 | Status pill placement | Before the `layout` group in every workspace (Animate, Model, Rig & Skin), not a workspace status group | — | B3 |
| M-AI-MVP-15 | Character setup tools | `model_inspect`, `model_fix`, `rig_create`, `library_list`, `library_use`; `project_import` of static models and Auto-Rig samples; `project_export` kinds `vrm`/`glb`/`omoba_kit` over `autorig.*` (MCP-SPEC §0.4.4); compositions forward the actor to inner runs | Operator-level access only (`operator_run autorig.*`): no single "rig this" step for the agent | B4 |
| M-AI-MVP-16 | `rig_create` confirm and grants (live) | Edit + View grants and a confirm card ("creates a skeleton and binds the skin: 2–3 undo steps"); headless: no confirm | No confirm: the user's scene changes active character without asking | B4 |
| M-AI-MVP-17 | Resume after close | Cut: a dropped socket ends the pairing; the agent pairs again | Resume: ≈ 1 day in B2 | phase C |
| M-AI-MVP-18 | Headless pictures | Deferred: headless `view_capture` answers `unsupported` with a hint; the schematic renderer stays a skeleton package | Headless agents work blind (numbers only) | phase C |
| M-AI-MVP-19 | Evals in the MVP | A smoke of 6 tasks (AE-01, -06, -13, -19, -24 and the new AE-R1 rig-and-walk) through an in-memory client ↔ server pair; the real-model gate is MVP-AC-5 (one recorded Claude Code session), not pass@1 over 25 tasks | Full P9 before release: ≈ 6 agent-days | release |
| M-AI-MVP-20 | LastRun and agent steps | An agent step never becomes `lastRun` and closes the user's Last-op card (`LAST_RUN_CLOSED.another`), as M6A-SPEC §5.7 asks of "another undoable operator" | — | — |
| M-AI-B5-1 | Bundle gates after B5 (`eager-diet`, branch `mai/b5-diet`) | **Gates unchanged; both routes green with headroom**: `/editor` 902,762 → **890,526 B** (gate 900,000 B, headroom 9,474 B; Auto-Rig eager-delta limit 897,736 B met), `/autorig` 664,644 → **651,721 B** (gate 660,000 B, headroom 8,279 B); both below `dev` (897,136 B and 659,116 B). Answers M-AI-MVP-2 without raising a gate ([wp-notes/mai-b5.md](wp-notes/mai-b5.md)) | Phase-B packages spend the headroom: B3's live status pill and B2's bridge must stay lazy | before merging `mai/port` |
| M-AI-B5-2 | Actor side of the run scope | The agent branches of the scope-ready operators (scope selection, character and clip, targets, empty-scope polls, the tween/simplify/match-range/mirror/convert agent checks) live in the lazy `operators/actorScope.ts`, loaded with the runner's actor rules (`actorRun.ts`) by `loadActorKit` (`operators/scope.ts`): the runner awaits it before an actor run's first poll, `loadAgentOperators` loads it with the agent operators, `createHeadlessEditorApp` installs it up front and the tool layer awaits it before its scope probe. User runs call the shipped `workflows/targets` rules again. An actor context polled before the kit loaded throws `unavailable` (no caller does) | Eager again: ≈ +0.5 kB on both routes | — |
| M-AI-B5-3 | Lazy operator bodies (`operators/builtin/lazyBodies.ts`) | `graph.*` (17), `pose.*` (6 of 7; `pose.unpinFeet` stays eager) and `clip.new`/`duplicate`/`delete`/`setSettings` keep their definitions and polls eager; their apply bodies moved unchanged to `graphBodies.ts`, `poseBodies.ts`, `clipBodies.ts`, loaded by each run's `prepare` (the module itself once loaded, so later runs stay synchronous up to `apply`, like `pose.mirrorAnimation`'s workflow, §5.27). Model, clip and project imports prefetch every body chunk (`preloadOperatorBodies`, intent). The first graph or pose run without a prior import waits for one chunk (a few ms); `pose.paste` reads the clipboard after loading the bodies | Eager again: ≈ +10.4 kB on both routes | — |
| M-AI-B5-4 | Lint rule descriptions | `io/src/lint/severities.ts` holds the rule ids and severities (the built-in profiles, `ruleSeverity`); `LINT_RULES` (`rules.ts`) adds the descriptions, which nothing on either first visit reads. Same table, same order | ≈ +1.3 kB on both routes | — |
| M-AI-B5-5 | Kept eager | The presence registry (synchronous `upsert` API, 0.5 kB), the runner's rule branches, the change-journal cap (a history unit test records `changes` without the kit), the doc store's restore host (a method over the store's private state) and the status pill with the Agent panel's lazy registration (B3's files) stay as ported: ≈ 2.2 kB together | Lazy presence: an async `upsert` or a race with the first tool call | phase C |
| M-AI-B5-6 | check:size boundaries | New lazy boundaries `actor-scope`, `graph-bodies`, `pose-bodies`, `clip-bodies` (markers only their chunks contain): a later eager import of them fails the gate | — | — |

| M-AI-B2-9 | Lockout | The fifth wrong code answers `not_found`; claims during the lockout `busy` | — | — |

### N.7 Phase B — B3 agent UI and live capture (2026-10-23)

Package B3 (`mai/b3-ui`), MCP-SPEC §0.4.1 items 4–5; build notes in [wp-notes/mai-b3.md](wp-notes/mai-b3.md).

| ID | Decision | Default chosen | Impact if changed | Decide by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M-AI-B3-1 | Session access and actions | The agent runtime (`agentRuntimeOf(app)`) follows B2's page session through `agent/bridge/slot.ts` (copied byte-identical from `mai/b2-bridge`, B2's file) and creates it with `loadAgentSession` when the panel opens (ports of `?agentPort=` preselected; connecting at start stays B2's e2e test API). The panel calls B2's actions (`select`, `cancel`, `accept`, `deny`, `setGrants`, `setPaused`, `answerConfirm`, `extra.claim`), typed optional (`AgentSessionControls`), so a missing one is drawn disabled | — | INT (B2 ↔ B3 merge) |
| M-AI-B3-2 | `view_capture` in the MVP | ≤ 6 frames (schema `frames` max 6; `keys`/`extremes` thinned evenly, `{every}` cut at 6 with a warning); views `user`, `front`, `back`, `left`, `right`, `top`, `three_quarter_left/right`; `shot`, `follow`, `fidelity: 'schematic'` and headless calls answer `unsupported` with a hint; contact marks and trails are not drawn (warning). Toolset version `1.0.0-dev.2` | INT sets `1.0.0-mvp.1` | INT |
| M-AI-B3-3 | Where the live capture service lives | The editor creates its services before the lazy agent module, so the service attaches per app (`attachViewCapture(app, service)` in agent-tools; `app.services.viewCapture` wins when set). Cameras in `agent-tools/src/tools/view/cameras.ts` (not `see/`), tiles in `viewport/src/manager/agentTile.ts` (`renderAgentTile`, synchronous), no schematic fallback: a render failure answers `unsupported` (retryable) | P5 moves cameras to `see/` and adds the fallback | phase C |
| M-AI-B3-4 | Audit row state | Computed in the UI from the history stacks (Undo at the top of the undo stack, Revert below it with a change record, "undone" on the redo stack, "can no longer be undone" when gone) plus the steps the panel reverted (`revertedBy`); the session's `reverted`/`unreachable` flags win when B2 sets them | — | — |
| M-AI-B3-5 | Asking before Revert all, the confirm card's focus | Revert all asks inline in the panel (a second button), not a modal; the confirm card never takes focus by itself (`Esc` on its buttons = Deny) so an agent call cannot catch the user's keystrokes | A modal or a focusing card: a few lines | owner review |
| M-AI-B3-6 | [Show] | `agent.show` (user-only: its poll refuses actor runs) queues `clip.setActive` + `time.setFrame {unit: 'clip'}` as user runs; unkeyed overrides on that character ask with the browser's confirm first; the 3 s outline of the touched controls waits for presence drawing (§0.4.2); switching to another character clears its selection as every clip switch does | — | phase C |
| M-AI-B3-7 | Status pill and lazy loading | The status line keeps the muted `AI` pill (eager, plus a 0.2 kB slot, `editor/src/agent/ui/slot.ts`) until the agent module loads; the Agent panel's chunk then puts the live pill there, which also hosts the confirm card, the grants dialog and the [Show] toast. `?agentPort=` opens the panel from the eager pill. `--presence-agent` is used with its `#e66cff` fallback (no token added to `packages/ui`) | A token in `tokens.json`: ui package change | INT |
| M-AI-B3-8 | e2e without the bridge | e2e builds expose `window.__EKZA_AGENT_E2E__` (only when the test API exists, a separate chunk) with a fake paired session that runs the real tool runtime (`editor/src/agent/ui/fakeSession.ts`); `ai-02-agent-panel.spec.ts` uses it, B2's `ai-01-live.spec.ts` covers the socket | — | — |

### N.8 Phase B — B4 rig tools (2026-10-23)

Package B4 (`mai/b4-rig`, MCP-SPEC §0.4.4): the character setup tools, their policy rows, the `rig_and_animate`
prompt and the actor forwarding of the Auto-Rig compositions. Notes and evidence: [`wp-notes/mai-b4.md`](wp-notes/mai-b4.md).

| ID | Decision | Default chosen | Impact if changed | Decide by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M-AI-B4-1 | The setup kinds of `project_import` / `project_export` (B1 owns the file tools) | A separate hook in `agent-tools/src/tools/setup/files.ts`: `SETUP_IMPORT` / `SETUP_EXPORT` (`handles(args)`, `run(ctx, args)`), their zod output shapes, and complete reference definitions `projectImportSetup` / `projectExportSetup` restricted to these kinds (same tool names; the B4 tests run them in place of B1's). B1 merges the input fields (samples `cube_lad`, `polybot_tpose`, `polybot_apose`; a static `.glb` path; kinds `vrm`, `glb`, `omoba_kit` with `meta`) and dispatches to the hook | Another seam: B1 rewrites the two runs | INT |
| M-AI-B4-2 | Headless samples and library | `SETUP_IMPORT` reads a sample through `services.readSample` with a `SampleDef` whose `file` is `cube-lad.glb`, `polybot-unskinned-tpose.glb` or `polybot-unskinned.glb` (ids outside `SampleId`; the server's reader must go by `file`), else runs `autorig.sample` (the tab's `/samples/`). The clip library fetches `<libraryPath>animations/…` with `globalThis.fetch`: the headless server must serve it (a fetch shim over the bundled `library/` folder); without it `library_list`/`library_use` answer `unsupported` | — | B1 |
| M-AI-B4-3 | Use Clip under an actor | `library.useClip` run by an actor with Edit access does not activate the new clip (`applyClipImport {activate: false}`; one line in the lazy `builtin/library.ts`, outside B4's directories); `library_use` passes `access: 'view'` headless (the clip becomes the active one, as `clip_create`'s) and `'edit'` live. The session keeper still shows a character's first clip | Activation live: needs the View grant | — |
| M-AI-B4-4 | Imports and rigging change what the user sees | `mesh.importStatic` selects the new meshes, Quick Rig and Bind Skin write the Rig & Skin session and select the new character's mesh: `project_import` (static) and `rig_create` run with `access: 'view'` and need Edit + View live; `rig_create` documents "the new character becomes the active, selected one; the playhead stays". A Quick Rig the user has open makes `rig_create` answer `busy` live (headless it is cancelled) | Actor-quiet imports: a change to `mesh.importStatic` (M6a) | — |
| M-AI-B4-5 | `rig_create` markers | `markers?: [{name, positionM: [x, y, z]}]` (≤ 9) instead of `'guess' \| Record` (the schema lint wants a unit suffix on every number; absent = the guess); side markers sit on the character's **right** side (x < 0), as Quick Rig places them; presets `standard` = Standard 33, `standard_fingers` = Full 52; `straightenToTPose` defaults to the guess's arm angle > 5° (AUTORIG §4.3), `rigidParts` to "several meshes" (AR-R4-3). The gates (start checks, ① Size AR-R5-1, arms AR-D10-3) are checked on the open Quick Rig before anything is written; a refusal names the `model_fix` call or the markers and closes the Quick Rig it opened | — | — |
| M-AI-B4-6 | `project_export` of a character | `meta: {name?, authors?, license?: cc0 \| cc-by-4.0 \| personal}` sets the VRM meta first (`char.setVrmMeta`, one step; no default licence, Q-SKIN-3); a blocking lint answers `poll_failed` with the lints in `detail` (no file); the result's artifact carries `delivered` and a read-back `{format, humanoidBones, joints, triangles, animations}`. No thumbnail headless (`vrm.thumbnail-square` stays a warning). Static model paths: `.glb` only (Auto-Rig's intake reads no `.gltf`/`.obj`) | — | B1 |
| M-AI-B4-7 | Catalogue and manifest | The five tools are appended after the stretch tools (`SETUP_TOOL_COUNT = 5`, group `setup`, `TOOL_NOUNS` + `model`, `library`), `rig_create` always loaded; toolset `1.0.0-dev.b4.1` (INT sets `1.0.0-mvp.1`) | — | INT |
| M-AI-B4-8 | Policy access of the confirm rows | `autorig.rig`, `autorig.sample`, `autorig.intake` are `confirm` with `access: 'view'` (one access per row; a View run is what the session rule needs). P3's `operator_run` must require Edit **and** View for them (`extraGrants`, `by_operator`) | — | P3 |
| M-AI-B4-9 | Test placement | The generic actor run of every allowed operator (`policy.test.ts`) and the envelope/invariant fuzz skip the lazy setup operators and tools (`SETUP_ROWS`, group `setup`); `setup-tools.test.ts` runs them under an actor with their session rules and validates their envelopes. MVP-AC-3 runs through the tool runtime in agent-tools and, as the same operator chain with glTF-Validator and vrm-validator, in `headless-tests/test/ai-rig.vrm.test.ts` (headless-tests may not depend on agent-tools without a `check-deps` table change) | One test through the runtime with both validators: an INT table change | INT |
| M-AI-B4-10 | OMOBA runtime check | `node tooling/omoba-validate.mjs` could not run: the omoba-bevy cargo caches (`target`, `target-b`) are symlinks to `/Volumes/Orico/omoba-build-cache/`, which is not mounted, and the disk rule forbids a third cache. The three VRMs are in `test-results/ai-rig/` (written by the headless test) | — | owner |

### N.9 Phase B — B1 server and headless (2026-10-23)

Package B1 (`mai/b1-server`, MCP-SPEC §0.4.1 item 2 and 8): the hub's routing, headless projects, the workspace
sandbox, the file and project tools, the MCP surface, the bundle, the evals smoke and MVP-AC-1. Notes and evidence:
[`wp-notes/mai-b1.md`](wp-notes/mai-b1.md).

| ID | Decision | Default chosen | Impact if changed | Decide by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M-AI-B1-1 | The hub ↔ bridge seam | B2's `TabLink` (`mcp-server/src/bridge/link.ts`, byte-identical to `mai/b2-bridge`); the hub takes it through `attachTabLink`/`tabLink()`: `claim` for `editor_connect`, `call` for `tab_…` routes, `unpair` for `project_close`, `tab()` for `projects_list`. Without `project` the hub routes to the only open project (the tab counts); several → `ambiguous` (§5.1) — B2's "the tab wins" (`bridge/route.ts`) is not used by this hub | — | INT |
| M-AI-B1-2 | Starting the bridge | `start.ts` looks `startHubBridge` up in `./bridge/server` and passes `{files: createTabFileHooks(hub.sandbox)}`; INT replaces the lookup with B2's `startHubBridge` from `./bridge/attach` | — | INT |
| M-AI-B1-3 | `project_open {project}` | v0.2's schema had no `project`, so the server could not tell "replace the tab's project" from "a new headless project": optional `project: tab_…` routes to the tab (it confirms); without it every call opens a new headless project | A separate live tool | INT |
| M-AI-B1-4 | Tab rows of `projects_list` | `TabInfo` carries neither the character count nor the dirty flag: the row says `characters: 0`, `dirty: false` | B2 adds both to `TabInfo` | phase C |
| M-AI-B1-5 | Extensions | Writes also allow `.zip` (the OMOBA kit of `project_export {kind: 'omoba_kit'}`); static models import as `.glb` only (Auto-Rig reads GLB; `.gltf`/`.obj` of §0.4.4 are not read) | — | — |
| M-AI-B1-6 | Character setup kinds | `project_import` (static samples, a static `.glb`) and `project_export` (`vrm`, `glb`, `omoba_kit`) hand the call to hooks the setup module registers (`registerFileToolHooks({import: SETUP_IMPORT, export: SETUP_EXPORT})`, B4); without them those kinds answer `unsupported`. The schemas already carry B4's fields (`meta`, `model` = a copy of B4's `modelReportOut`, the export artifact with `delivered` and a read-back union, `character`) | One line at INT; the copied `modelOut` replaced by the import | INT |
| M-AI-B1-7 | Bundled files | Besides Polybot and its two unskinned GLBs the package bundles the Cube Lad (MIT, 11 kB; §0.4.4 said `--samples-dir`) and the clip library's starter pack (CC0, 624 kB), which the server serves through its own `globalThis.fetch`: `/library/animations/<file>` from disk, every other request refused (the no-network rule of §5.19 T15, enforced). The original fetch comes back when the last hub closes | Cube Lad through `--samples-dir` only | publish |
| M-AI-B1-8 | Confirmations of the file tools | Live only (replace the project, import a path or a static model into a non-empty scene, a download, an overwrite); headless calls need no `confirm: true` | — | — |
| M-AI-B1-9 | Toolset version | `1.0.0-dev.b1.1` on this branch (two manifest changes); INT sets `1.0.0-mvp.1` | — | INT |
| M-AI-B1-10 | Evals smoke | The six tasks run on Polybot (AE-01, -06, -19, -24) and Seed-san (AE-13) through the in-process pair, each with adversarial scripts that must fail; graders are copies of P2's test graders on raw bone worlds of the saved result. AE-R1 runs once B4's tools are listed; its script mirrors B4's `rigAndAnimateScript` (TODO INT: import it) | — | INT |
| M-AI-B1-11 | `lastop-*.js` (M-AI-MVP-13) | Not reproduced with rolldown 1.2.12 on this branch; the build now fails when `dist/` holds anything but `cli.mjs` and its map, or when the bin imports a package at run time; target `node22` | — | — |
| M-AI-B1-12 | Trash file names | `.ekza-trash/<yyyymmddThhmmss.mmmZ>-<name>`, `-2`, `-3`… on a collision | — | — |
| M-AI-B1-13 | Live resources | `selection` and `time` of the tab are taken from the user part of its `scene_summary` (one bridge call, no separate read) | A bridge read per resource | phase C |
| M-AI-B1-14 | Package contents | `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`, `README.md` and `LICENSE` listed in `files` are not generated yet (the npm publish is deferred, §0.4.2) | — | publish |

### N.10 MVP integration (INT, 2026-10-24)

Branch `mai/int` (from `mai/port`): B5, B1, B2, B3, B4 merged in that order with merge commits; notes and evidence in
[`wp-notes/mai-int.md`](wp-notes/mai-int.md), the recorded Claude Code session in
[`evidence/m-ai-mvp/`](evidence/m-ai-mvp/).

| ID | Decision | Default chosen | Impact if changed | Decide by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M-AI-INT-1 | Routing | B1's hub routing (by `project`; none → the only open target, the paired tab counts; several → `ambiguous`; a gone `tab_…` → `expired_handle`) replaces B2's "the tab wins"; `bridge/route.ts` removed; the paired tab is a `projects_list` row; the client's name from `initialize` (`hub.setClientInfo`) names the actor in `hello.ok`, the grants dialog and the panel | — | — |
| M-AI-INT-2 | Bridge start and workspace transfers | `start.ts` calls B2's `startHubBridge(hub, {files: createTabFileHooks(hub.sandbox)})` (hooks only when the sandbox is on); the bridge pre-attaches workspace bytes and performs a tab's workspace writes **only while the tab's grants include Files and Workspace** (a write without them answers `not_granted`) | — | — |
| M-AI-INT-3 | Setup kinds of the file tools | `registerFileToolHooks({import: SETUP_IMPORT, export: SETUP_EXPORT})` in `tools/catalogue.ts`; `files/defs.ts` uses B4's `modelReportOut`; B4's reference `projectImportSetup`/`projectExportSetup` dropped; setup exports go to `exports/<file>` like the clip exports (the live bridge derives the same target) | — | — |
| M-AI-INT-4 | AE-R1 | B4's `rigAndAnimateScript` moved to `agent-tools/src/text/rigAndAnimate.ts` (exported); the evals smoke runs it, AE-R1 is no longer skipped (reference and adversarial) | — | — |
| M-AI-INT-5 | Toolset and reference | `AGENT_TOOLSET_VERSION` `1.0.0-mvp.1`; 37 tools as §0.4.3, `alwaysLoad` on 12 (the eleven of Q-AI-37 with `view_capture`, plus `rig_create`); `docs/agent-tools-reference.md` generated from the manifest (`pnpm --filter @ekza-studio/agent-tools docs`, checked by `test/reference.test.ts`) | — | — |
| M-AI-INT-6 | Agent panel placement | The panel opened from the pill or `?agentPort=` docks **right**, beside the Channels panel (`EXTRA_PANEL_SLOTS` in `shell/layout.ts`); B3's default (`center`) put it over the viewport, so the user could not watch the agent; `?agentPort=` opens it once dockview is bound (it failed silently before) | `center`: the viewport hides behind the panel | owner review |
| M-AI-INT-7 | Revert all | Also takes back the inner steps of a call (a composition's runs: `rig_create`, imports, `library_use {name}`; the audit row names only the last step), found by the pairing's actor id. When the agent's steps are the top of the undo stack and one carries no change record (imports, rigging) or `edit.revertSteps` fails, they are **undone** one by one instead (the user's steps are below them; Redo brings the agent's work back) | A revert step that restores rigging: `edit.revertSteps` failed on Quick Rig's control removal order ("controls/… still references it") — a kernel fix for phase C | phase C |
| M-AI-INT-8 | Test API | B3's e2e hook registers its fake session as the page's session (`setAgentSession`), so `agent()` sees both the live and the fake session; the panel's session reports `build: 'e2e'` in e2e builds; `ai-01-live` pairs by clicking `agent-allow` in the grants dialog | — | — |
| M-AI-INT-9 | Hosted editor origin | B2 compiled `https://studio.ekza.io` into the bridge's allow-list as "the hosted editor" (M-AI-B2-4), but NAME-2 makes `studio.ekza.io` the publishing site (Ekza Hub) | Another editor URL: one constant in `bridge/server.ts` and `docs/agents.md` | **owner**, before the release |
| M-AI-INT-10 | MVP-AC-5 method | `tooling/m-ai-ac5.mjs` runs `claude -p --input-format stream-json --permission-prompt-tool stdio` with an `--mcp-config` for the built server and plays the user through Playwright: Connect in the panel, the code into the first message, Claude Code's permission prompt for `editor_connect` (it carries `requiresUserInteraction`, so an `--allowedTools` rule does not cover it) answered Allow for `mcp__ekza-studio__*` only, the grants dialog (Edit, Files, Workspace, View) and every confirm card; evidence scrubbed of local paths. Recorded run 2026-10-24: 24 turns, 2 confirm cards, 3 contact sheets, `exports/cube-lad.vrm` (glTF-Validator 0 errors, 33 humanoid bones). The editor was `next start` of the e2e-enabled build opened **without** `?e2e=1` (production behaviour) | Re-run on a plain production build before the release | owner accepts |
| M-AI-INT-11 | Bundle after INT | Gates unchanged; `/editor` **890,932 B** (gate 900,000 B, headroom 9,068 B; below the Auto-Rig eager limit 897,736 B), `/autorig` **651,856 B** (gate 660,000 B, headroom 8,144 B): +406 B / +135 B over B5 (the pill slot, B2's export registrations, the panel dock rule). Answers M-AI-MVP-2 and the bundle decision of §0.3 | — | — |

## O. Third-party trademarks (2026-10-24)

| ID | Decision | Owner choice | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| TM-1 | **No third-party product names** in the UI, docs, code (comments, identifiers, test names), package metadata or file names. Behaviour is described by what it does ("Alt+drag navigation", "industry-standard DCC hotkeys", "weighted/stepped tangent model"); research notes name compared products neutrally ("a leading commercial desktop DCC", "a popular open-source DCC", "a web auto-rigging service"). Open formats (glTF, VRM, FBX, BVH) are named to state compatibility, libraries in credits and licence notices. Git history is not rewritten. | Chosen by the owner (the repository is public; avoid any objection from a vendor) | Allowed remaining spellings, each for a reason: the AnimX port's Apache-2.0 attribution (`packages/anim-core/NOTICE`, `LICENSE`, `REUSE.toml`, the per-file headers of `packages/anim-core/src/curve/animx/*.ts`, the generated THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES); the AnimX oracle's upstream repository URL and the library's own build define (`tooling/animx-oracle/generate.mjs`, `oracle.cpp`) and the goldens' `source` field they generate; the functional clip/node-name marker of the auto-rigging service in `packages/io/src/gltf/provenance.ts` (`NON_REDISTRIBUTABLE_NAME`, tests build their names from it); the old ids in `packages/editor-core/src/prefs/legacy.ts` (TM-2, TM-4); binary fixtures and the bone names of their truth file (`fixtures/models/polybot-truth.json`). A case-insensitive `git grep` for the removed names lists only these (and the binary fixtures). |
| TM-2 | **Renamed persisted ids, migrated on read**: `navProfile` → `dcc` (Alt + three-button mouse) and `oneButton`; `spaceBehaviour` → `maximize`; `modeling.primitiveDefaults` → `dcc`; `rigging.orientPreset` and the Orient Joint `preset` (also `skel.joint.create {orient}`) → `xAim` (X down the bone, Y up) and `yAim` (Y down the bone, Z up); keymap preset id → `dcc` (`keymap/dcc.ts`, `DCC_KEYMAP`). | Default (no owner input needed) | Preferences saved by earlier builds load through `migrateLegacyPrefs` and are written back with the new ids; operator arguments (key bindings, option-box defaults, scripts, agent calls) pass `migrateLegacyArgs` in the runner before validation; the e2e URL parameters `nav`/`space` likewise. No project, autosave or VRMA format stored these ids, so the document schema is unchanged. Test ids: `firstrun-nav-dcc`, `firstrun-space-maximize`. Tests: `packages/editor-core/test/kernel/prefs-legacy.test.ts`, `packages/editor/test/test-api-legacy.test.ts`. |
| TM-3 | Agent toolset **`1.0.0-mvp.2`**: two tool descriptions reworded (`pose_key` `syncRotation`, the static-model import hint), no schema change. | Default | Manifest and `docs/agent-tools-reference.md` regenerated (§5.21). |
| TM-4 | **Neutral feature names**: TM-1 also covers the names of features specific to one vendor's DCC. The channel list panel is **Channels** (panel title and Windows menu; prose "the Channels panel"); that vendor's Space-hold full menu is a **Quick Menu** (research notes only: the product's Space-hold pane/camera radial and command palette keep their names); its modeling tool panel is **Modeling Tools** (the Model workspace's tab keeps its title **Toolkit**; the disabled "Primitive Options…" item says "Primitive options are in the Toolkit (⋯)"); its node-based material editor is a **shader editor** in prose, and the command-palette alias of `palette.assignMaterial` is "Shader Editor". Generic terms stay: Graph Editor, Dope Sheet, Outliner, Attribute Editor, marking menu, timeline, time slider, tangents, IK/FK; FBX as a format name. Identifiers: panel kind and key scope `channels` (`PANEL.channels`), `RIG_SCOPE.channels` = `rig.channels`, `ChannelsPanel`, `CHANNELS_PANEL`, `registerChannelsPanel`, `editor/src/panels/channels/` (with the shared `kit/`), test ids `channels-panel` and the dock tab's `panel-channels`, e2e helpers `showChannelsPanel` and `keyWithChannelsPanel`. | Chosen by the owner (follow-up to TM-1) | **Migrated on read** (legacy table fields `panelKind` and `keyScope`), written back with the new ids only: saved dock layouts and their focus snapshots (`ekza-animator:layout:*`; `migrateLegacyLayout` in `shell/layout.ts` maps the `panels` keys, each panel's id, component and `params.kind`, the groups' `views` and `activeView`, `<kind>#<n>` instance ids included), user layout presets (`ekza-animator:presets:*`), `Prefs.keymapOverrides` (each binding's scope and arguments, `migrateLegacyPrefs`) and the `panel` argument of `workspace.showPanel`, `view.tool` and `view.toggleMaximize` (`migrateLegacyArgs` in the runner: key bindings, menus, scripts, agent calls). No layout or preset schema version changes; no project, autosave or VRMA format stores a panel kind, so the document schema is unchanged. Kept on purpose: the `cb-*` test ids and `--cb-*` colour tokens and the `FR-CB-*` requirement ids (a two-letter prefix and stable trace ids, not a name), the `mtk-*` test ids (the Model workspace's Toolkit, as `rtk-*` is the Rig Toolkit's). Agent toolset **`1.0.0-mvp.3`**: the `pose_read` and `pose_set` descriptions and the `space` parameter's description reworded, no schema change; manifest and `docs/agent-tools-reference.md` regenerated (§5.21). Tests: `packages/editor-core/test/kernel/prefs-legacy.test.ts`, `packages/editor/test/layout-legacy.test.tsx`. |

## P. Ekza Studio touch shell (Halo), defaults chosen (2026-10-24)

Defaults and owner questions of the touch-first shell ([STUDIO-TOUCH-SPEC](STUDIO-TOUCH-SPEC.md) v0.2,
[ADR 0023](adr/0023-studio-touch-ui.md), Proposed). v0.2 followed a review of v0.1 by three critics (75 issues, spec
§26); rows marked *(v0.2)* were changed or added by it.

- Every row has a default, so that work can start.
- Rows marked **owner** are the spec's open questions (§24, OQ-*). The default stands until the owner answers.
- **ST-B1** is filled in by T1a, T1–T3, T5c and T12 with measured numbers, as AR-B1 was.
- Build-time decisions are added by the packages as `ST-T<n>-*` rows and consolidated by T12 as `ST-INT-*`.

| ID | Question | Default chosen | Impact if changed | Decide by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ST-1 | Name, internal id, route, package | UI **"Ekza Studio"**, presented as one studio with two layouts; `/editor` titled **"Ekza Studio Pro"** (UI strings only; id `studio`, routes and `ekza-animator:` keys unchanged). New shell: id `halo`, route `/app`, package `@ekza-studio/halo`, marker `ekza-halo-shell`, e2e family `ha-`. Avoid `/studio` while `to:'studio'` means `/editor`, and avoid the id `simple` (OQ-1) | Renaming the id or route after T2 touches the unions, the entry choice, the size tables and the e2e specs | **owner**, T2 |
| ST-2 *(v0.2)* | The entry at `/` | **A first visit opens Ekza Studio's Home on every device**; no two-card chooser (it read as Lite against Pro). A remembered choice wins (`'halo'` added; stored `'studio'` / `'autorig'` keep redirecting); the layout switch updates it; `/?chooser=1` keeps a three-row chooser. Behind `HALO_ENTRY = false` until T12 (OQ-2) | Keeping the chooser for desktop first visits: one branch in `REDIRECT_SCRIPT` + the entry contract | **owner**, T12 |
| ST-3 | Stages | The Path: Make (`model`), Paint (`model` + Paint Colour), Rig (`rig`, segments Character / Skeleton / Skin), Animate (`animate`); Share is a sheet, not a stage. Stages never gate; missing prerequisites show bridging empty states | Merging Paint into Make would hide the colour door from beginners (simplicity review) | T6a |
| ST-4 | Destination default | **"Just exploring"** (Mesh Check errors only); asked on the first line of the Rig-it card and in Share's header; the destination maps to the existing export profiles | Defaulting to OMOBA would warn on every non-OMOBA project | T11 |
| ST-5 *(v0.2)* | Input routing | **Shared** until the first `pointerType: 'pen'` event, then **Pencil edits, fingers move the view**, with a *Keep / Change* toast; *Pencil only* is opt-in. In Shared mode a finger drag manipulates only what is selected, a handle, or (with a brush) the mesh after 10 pt or 120 ms; anywhere else it orbits. Palm rejection while the pen is down or hovering (500 ms); contact-size rejection only if device check D14 confirms usable sizes; pressure read only for pens. Stored as `halo.input`, never in Pro's `navProfile` | Claiming every mesh hit (v0.1) makes one-finger orbit impossible in close-ups | T6a |
| ST-6 *(v0.2)* | Gesture set | One and two fingers only; two-finger tap = undo, **no hold-to-repeat**; no three-, four- or five-finger gestures; redo in the toast and the History ribbon; quick pinch = frame all (on, with a toggle); **double-tap frames for every input** (grow and fill are chips); *Reduce gestures* setting | Three-finger redo can be added if device check D10 shows WebKit delivers it reliably | T6a |
| ST-7 | Long-press | 450 ms with ≤ 10 pt movement opens the Halo ring or a row's menu; a press that moves first is a drag; Pencil hover-hold never opens the ring (hover only previews); no reliance on `contextmenu` | — | T1 |
| ST-8 *(v0.2)* | Pick tolerances and slop | Touch 22 px pick / 10 px slop / 450 ms multi-tap / 24 px double-tap and cycle radius; pen 9 / 4 / 350 ms / 8 px; mouse unchanged; manipulators scale by pointer type at render time with hysteresis (never through `tools.manipulatorSize`); the Loupe is a list of named chips (no magnifier in v1) | Tuning on device (D5) | T1 |
| ST-9 *(v0.2)* | Modifiers | One **Thumb key** on the iPad Shift-key model: tap = next action only, double-tap = lock, hold = momentary, slide up = chooser. Meanings split into a **tap line** (Select more, Pick or unpick, Unselect, Fill part, Sample value, Snap point) and a **drag line** (Fine, Smart, Alone, Snap, Falloff size, Centre, Brush size, Band, Copy, Carry pose, Axis), each with its own glyph; hidden when it means nothing and after the first hardware modifier. The source carries `{shift, mod, alt, held, button}`; a lock is the same state as the matching tray toggle | Separate always-visible latch chips (canvas concept) cost space and add abstract nouns | T6a |
| ST-10 *(v0.2)* | Auto-key in Ekza Studio | **Rec is Halo's own setting `halo.rec`, default on**, applied as a `shell`-layer override of `autoKey` while Halo is mounted and an animation is active, removed on unmount; Pro's stored Auto Key is never written. No question card, no "not keyed" pill, one Key petal (OQ-4) | v0.1's "ask once" adds a modal question before the first pose | **owner**, T7 |
| ST-11 *(v0.2)* | Halo on desktop click | With a **fine pointer and a keyboard**, the Halo opens on **right-click or hold**; a click selects and the breadcrumb shows ⋯ Verbs. Touch-first devices open it on every tap. Settings › Input › *Halo opens on* switches | Every click opening six petals is noisy for desktop-DCC users | T6a |
| ST-12 | Deep tools in the release | Touch Keys (Dope Sheet), Curves (Graph Editor), Channels and Weights are **release requirements**; *Open in the Pro layout* is a convenience only | Deferring Curves to Pro leaves the top tasks 14–15 without a touch path (parity review) | T12 |
| ST-13 *(v0.2)* | Rig it v1 | All in Halo: `skel.quickRig.start` + `guess`, the dots drawn on the canvas, **native *Adjust dots*** (drag, Re-guess, Mirror, Remove, Pick, Front · Side), *Rig it* through `editor-core/autorigFlow`, and *Skeleton only* (`skel.quickRig.commit`). No hand-off to Auto-Rig ② (OQ-6) | The v0.1 hand-off sent beginners into a third UI mid-journey | T9 |
| ST-14 | Multi-pane in Halo | View sheet panes 1 · 2 · 4 on Wide containers, 1 · 2 on Regular, 1 with a switcher on Compact; main pane `pane-single`, side inset `pane-side` (Pro's ids, so cameras carry over) | — | T6a |
| ST-15 | Deliberate Pro hand-offs | Only (a) Pro's docking chrome (dock, presets, chrome toggles, Pro focus mode), (b) many editors visible at once, (c) keymap editing; their operators stay runnable from search ("Opens in the Pro layout"). Nothing goes to Auto-Rig | Each extra entry needs a written reason in `proOnly.ts` | T6b |
| ST-16 *(v0.2)* | Parameters of operators without ParamDefs | **Halo-only forms** (`@ekza-studio/editor-core/forms`, typed against `OperatorMap`), shown as pre-run forms with ✓; after-run Echo chips only for `adjustable` operators. Pro, `LastRun` and the MCP schemas never read the forms. Halo's own round trip through Pro restores from its session mirror, not from this | One generic `handoffState` with a union result would change Auto-Rig's contract | — (T2, frozen) |

### P · T3 — host re-homing and the Pro switch (2026-10-24)

Branch `halo/t3-host`; notes in [`wp-notes/halo-t3.md`](wp-notes/halo-t3.md).

| ID | Decision | Default chosen | Impact if changed | Decide by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ST-T3-1 | How the host registers what only Pro registered (§20.3 item 12) | `host/registrations`: an eager seam (`index.ts` + `recovery.ts`) that loads **one** lazy `parts` chunk at once; it registers `view.selectionMask` and `view.toggleFullscreen`, then Model's marking-menu definitions on the first Model entry and Rig & Skin's menu items, `rig.modes`, `rig.snap.projectedCenter` and `rig.jointSize.set` on the first Rig & Skin entry (a chunk each). Once per app, idempotent (`registerOnce` skips registered ids), kept across shell switches, removed with the host. Pro keeps its pane overlays, status groups, dock tabs and `watchRigViewOnly` | One async loader per part cost `/autorig` +333 B (Turbopack's loader ≈ 110 B raw each); eager registration would add the view operators and menus to `/autorig` and `/app`. Pro's `model.modes` / `model.convert` now arrive with the first Model entry, together with the marking-menu controller (no press opened a menu before that either) | T12 |
| ST-T3-2 | The Recovery hook (J10) | The host times the offer (`watchRecovery`: once per page, slots present, no dialog, after the first-run dialog of a shell that opens one, never on a handoff page); the mounted shell provides how: `provideRecoveryHook(app, {firstRun, offer})`, latest wins. Pro: `firstRun: params.from === null` (T2's P6), offer = the Recovery dialog; Halo: `firstRun: false`, Home › Continue (T11); Auto-Rig: none (its own card). Not a `MountedShell` member, so T2's frozen contract is unchanged | A `MountedShell.recovery?` member would tie the hook to mounting (and save ≈ 60 B) but changes a frozen contract | T11 |
| ST-T3-3 | `watchRigViewOnly` | Stays in Pro's lazy rig chunk (`shell/rig/register.ts`); the host's rig part never calls `setRigViewOnly`; unit-tested at a narrow window with and without Pro | — | — |
| ST-T3-4 | Pro's switch | `shell.halo` (internal) with a lazy `haloSwitch.ts`; Help › and File › *Switch to the Simple Layout*; a status-line *▤ Simple layout* in its own group after the AI pill. **Visible on `halo/int` now**: ST-30's "`/app` by URL only until T12" holds for users because `halo/int` reaches `dev` only at T12 | If Halo work ever merges to `dev` before T12, gate the two items and the button with `PRO_TITLE` (three one-line conditions) | T12 |
| ST-T3-5 | The remembered entry on a switch (§1.2) | Pro → Simple rewrites a remembered `'studio'` to `'halo'`; Ask and `'autorig'` stay; behind `HALO_ENTRY` (no write until T12). Halo → Pro's reverse write is T6a's | Writing for Ask too would make the switch choose the user's front door | T12 |
| ST-T3-6 | The "Ekza Studio Pro" title | `PRO_TITLE = false` in `shell/menuItems.ts`; Pro's menu-bar mark reads `PRO_NAME`. The `/editor` document title (`apps/web`, "Editor · Ekza Studio") flips with it at T12 (patch P-T3-3) | — | T12 |
| ST-T3-7 | UI wrappers (T5a's patch, ST-T5A-4) | Registered by the host's lazy `parts` chunk when the page is idle (`requestIdleCallback`, 3 s timeout; 200 ms timer where it is missing), on every route; `/editor` +0 B for it | Wiring into `lazyOperatorChunk` instead costs eager bytes on both routes | T12 |
| ST-T3-8 | T3's measured delta (ST-B1 input) | Production build, gzip -9, against `halo/int` `9d7b3a09` (T0 and T5b merged): `/editor` 895,135 → 894,422 B (**−713 B**: Pro's eager menus and view operators went lazy, the switch items came in), `/autorig` 655,718 → 655,922 B (**+204 B**: the seam, the Recovery watcher and T0's hide flush), `/app` +198 B. Against `154e7188` before T0: −795 B / +152 B. Within T3's proposed 256 B (+128 B for the switch items) | Counts against ST-B1's 2,048 B total (here it gives some back on `/editor`) | **owner**, with ST-B1 |
| ST-T3-9 | The hide flush (T0's patch, S24) | `startShellAutosave` writes the snapshot on `pagehide` and on `visibilitychange` to hidden **only while the project is dirty**; `beforeunload`'s clean stays | Writing clean work too (T0's line as given) leaves this page's snapshot as an orphan on a desktop after `beforeunload` removed it, so the next load offers Recovery for a clean project; on iOS T0's store keeps the snapshot anyway | T0, T12 |

### P · T5a — forms, lint and wrappers (2026-10-24)

Branch `halo/t5a-forms`; notes in [`wp-notes/halo-t5a.md`](wp-notes/halo-t5a.md).

| ID | Decision | Default chosen | Impact if changed | Decide by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ST-T5A-1 | Wrapper names | `pose.hold` (Hold Pose), `pose.pasteAt` (Paste Pose at Frame), `pose.placeGoal` (Place IK Goal), `pose.lookAt` (Look At), `pose.reach` (Reach), `clip.loop` (Make Loop), `key.scaleValues` (Scale Key Values), `key.offsetValues` (Offset Key Values), `key.offsetInLoop` (Offset in Loop), `imagePlane.replace` (Replace Image): the spec's ids, Pro-style labels for More and search | Renaming later touches the manifest, forms and policy rows | T12 |
| ST-T5A-2 | How a wrapper calls the wrapped operator | Its poll asks the wrapped poll; its `prepare` runs the wrapped `prepare` and full poll; its `apply` runs the wrapped `apply` in the wrapper's one transaction and annotates the wrapper's label. One step under the wrapper's label (also for override-only runs). Agent runs refused in the poll too | A dispatcher (`forward` from `prepare`) would record the wrapped operator's step and label, and `imagePlane.replace` would be two steps | — |
| ST-T5A-3 | Argument types | Declared in `uiWrapperChunk.ts` by declaration merging into `OperatorMap`; `args.ts` (T2's contract file) untouched | Folding them into `args.ts` is a line patch, no behaviour change | T2 |
| ST-T5A-4 | Registration | `loadUiWrappers(app)`: idempotent per app, never disposed, loads the M-AI operators first; not in Pro's eager registration nor in `lazyOperatorChunk`, so `/editor` gains 0 B. The host calls it at idle (T3) | Wiring it into `lazyOperatorChunk` lets any surface run a wrapper id before the load, for a few dozen eager bytes on `/editor` and `/autorig` | T3, T12 |
| ST-T5A-5 | Session defaults | Clip = the active one; frame = the playhead (the active clip only, else required); time range = the band; key targets = selected keys → selected channels → animated channels of selected controls; positions in character space (`pose.goals`' space) | — | — |
| ST-T5A-6 | Reach and Rec | `key` defaults to the effective auto key (Halo's Rec override): keyed through `pose.goals`, else unkeyed edits through `pose.setAt {propose}`, one step either way; `solveReach` is the preview. Hands and feet only: head and hips need a spine solve `pose.goals` lacks | Head / hips Reach: Look At, a Move, or a new kernel solve (ADR) | T7 |
| ST-T5A-7 | An FK limb under Place Goal / Reach | Switched to IK over the time band only (`ensureIk: 'auto'`); without a band, refused with a pointer to *Hand leads* / *Foot leads*; Reach without Rec refuses an FK limb | Switching at a single frame would bend the neighbouring frames | T7 |
| ST-T5A-8 | Replace Image | Delete + import + settings in one step; keeps name, view, offset, size, opacity, depth and visibility; the plane gets a new id and is drawn last among its view's planes (`imagePlane.set` has no order); a locked plane is refused | Keeping the id or order needs a new kernel field | — |
| ST-T5A-9 | `FormDef` beyond the §20.4 sketch | Dotted param names into object arguments (`tolerance.rotationRad`, `params.speed`) and `base` (fixed starting values); `fromSession` stays `'time' \| 'target'` (playhead or band; selection, active animation, picked target, copied pose) | — | — |
| ST-T5A-10 | Which operators have forms | 20: `graph.curves.simplify`, `graph.infinity.set`, `key.tween`, `time.insertTime`, `time.deleteTime`, `pose.mirrorAnimation`, `clip.new`, `rig.ikFk.switch`, `rig.ikFk.matchRange`, `library.useClip` and the ten wrappers. Defaults are the operator's own; non-adjustable operators with ParamDefs use those (`formOf` → `source: 'params'`) | More forms are additive (one entry each, param-lint checks them) | T6b–T9 |
| ST-T5A-11 | param-lint | Runs in `test:tooling`; loads the TypeScript registry through a `typescript` transpile hook; the surface checks read `PARAM_SURFACES` from `packages/halo/src/manifest/paramSurfaces.ts` once T6b exports it; "a Pro option box" = a public operator with visible scalar ParamDefs (48 today) | — | T6b |
| ST-T5A-12 | Turbo inputs of `test:tooling` | Needs `packages/*/src/**` (param-lint reads sources); not changed in T5a (shared file) | Without it a cached pass can hide a source change | T12 |
| ST-T0-1 | Effective-preferences API before T2's contract | `overridePrefs(app.prefs, 'device' \| 'shell', patch) → Disposer` in `editor-core/src/prefs/overrides.ts` (exported from the package root), not yet the method `app.prefs.override` (the `EditorApp` type is T2's; the one-line patch is in `wp-notes/halo-t0.md`). Patches are **deep** (a nested object merges field by field; arrays replace). While an override is in place a write of an overridden field lands in the base and shows once the override goes; a write that carries the shown value back (`{ ...getState().modeling, gridStep }`) keeps the base's own value. The layer patches the store's `setState` at the first override and restores it with the last, so a page without overrides runs the untouched store | Typing it on `EditorApp.prefs` changes `types.ts` and `kernel.ts` (T2) | T0 |
| ST-T0-2 | Memory profile: device class and numbers | iOS WebKit = an iPhone/iPad user agent, or "Macintosh" with `maxTouchPoints > 1` (iPadOS Safari's desktop agent); input routing never uses it. Megapixels at the **native** ratio over the larger of screen and window: DPR range `[1, 1.5]` above 4 MP, MSAA off above 5 MP. The budgets **96 MB undo (which counts journal bytes) and a 24 MB journal cap** (from 256 + 64, the same 4 : 1 split). Decided at the GL host's first render (before the context; on every route); `?memoryProfile=ios\|standard` forces it in e2e builds. A device that has a lower stored budget gets 96 (the layer sets a value, not a cap) | Tuning on devices (D7, D8); a cap instead of a value needs a computed layer | T0, device gate |
| ST-T0-3 | Texture cap mechanism | Lower `renderer.capabilities.maxTextureSize` to 2048 (`viewport/src/gl/textureCap.ts`): three.js resizes an image, canvas or bitmap above the limit while it uploads it. Compressed (KTX2) and data textures are not resized. Proven in ha-00: a 4096-wide texture is drawn at 2048 and the baked VRM is byte-identical with the profile on and off | A resize in the loader would change what Pro shows on desktop too | T0 |
| ST-T0-4 | Where iOS files wait | `FileService.download()` on iOS WebKit (with Web Share for files) turns into a SaveTicket in the page's save queue and shows the host's save prompt; the prompt mounts in its own root on `<body>` unless a host renders `<SavePrompt>` in its tree; if its chunk cannot load the file downloads. Every export, MCP export and project save of every shell goes through it without call-site changes. Share timeout **10 s → `<a download>`** (if the user is still in the sheet, the file may also land in Downloads; D11 tunes it); a dismissed sheet (`AbortError`) leaves the ticket ready; any other refusal downloads. Prompt text: "*name* is ready · Save to Files", then "Shared · iPad does not confirm where it went" or "Saved to Downloads" | Mounting the prompt in `StudioHost` (optional patch) | T0, T11 |
| ST-T0-5 | iOS autosave snapshot | The autosave store keeps the page's **own** snapshot through every clean end on iOS (its `remove` of the own slot is a no-op there); the next write replaces it, and after a reload it is an orphan offered like any other. Other slots (recovered, discarded, housekeeping) are removed as before. The flush on `pagehide` / `visibilitychange` is a T3 line patch (`shell/autosave.ts`) | Keeping it only after a share would need the share state in the store | T0, T3 |
| ST-T0-6 | `accept` policy | `pickerAccept(list)` returns `undefined` on iOS WebKit, so the four model/project/animation pickers carry no `accept` there; the image-plane picker keeps its image list (iOS maps image types). Sniffing (`sniffFileKind`, `nameForKind`, `UNSUPPORTED_FILE_TEXT`) ships in `services/fileTypes.ts`; routing Pro's file pipeline through it is a patch for the unowned `shell/files.ts` | — | T0 |
| ST-T0-7 | Touch projects in the local gate | The default local `playwright test` runs `chrome`, `chrome-touch` and `webkit-ipad`; the touch projects run the `ha-*` family only (T1 adds `ar-*`). Playwright 1.63 expects WebKit build 2359; without it the config launches the newest installed build (`webkit-2370` here, as the design prototypes did) — nothing is downloaded; `EKZA_WEBKIT_PATH` overrides, `EKZA_E2E_WEBKIT=0` leaves WebKit out. CI keeps SwiftShader alone | A separate WebKit script would keep `pnpm run ci` Chrome-only but let the WebKit check rot | T0 |
| ST-T0-8 | Web manifest | `id` and `start_url` `/app`, `scope` `/` (so Halo can open `/editor` and `/autorig` inside the Home Screen app), `display: standalone` (iOS has no `fullscreen` display), theme and background `#1b1c1f`; icons 192, 512, maskable 512 and the 180 px `apple-touch-icon.png` at the root, rendered from `app/icon.svg`. Linked only from the `/app` layout (T2) | `scope: '/app'` would send Pro and Auto-Rig links out to Safari | T0, T2 |
| ST-T0-9 | Shared-file touches outside the T0 row | Three additive barrel edits so that the row's own modules can be used across packages: `editor-core/src/index.ts` (two export lines: the override API, `basePrefs`), `viewport/src/index.ts` (`applyTextureCap`), `editor/src/host/index.ts` (`pickerAccept` for Auto-Rig's one-line edits). One new file beside the row's: `editor/src/host/deviceOverride.ts`, the lazy chunk that lays the memory profile's patch (so the override layer is not in the eager bundle) | The integration agent may move them | T12 |
| ST-T0-10 | Bundle cost of T0 | Eager on every route: the memory-profile decision, the texture cap, the iOS check and the accept policy, the file service's share routing and lazily backed `save()`, the autosave keep rule and the base registry. The ticket code, the share platform, the save prompt and the override layer are lazy chunks. Measured **`/editor` 893,421 B (+1,631 B over dev's 891,790 B), `/autorig` 654,180 B (+1,652 B over 652,528 B)**; T0 had no ST-B1 allowance, so the owner or T12 decides whether it counts against the 2,048 B total | Moving more of it lazy needs an async profile decision (MSAA is fixed at context creation) | **owner**, T12 |
| ST-B1-T1 | **T1's share of ST-B1, measured** (production build, `check:size`, gzip -9; `dev` @ `7af76d0d` = `/editor` 891,790 B, `/autorig` 652,528 B) | **T1 ≤ 768 B on each route** (option (b) of the T1a proposal, the main session's default while OQ-3 is open). Measured by the green gate at `cdb7e060`: **`/editor` 892,512 B (+722 B), `/autorig` 653,279 B (+751 B)**, both within 768 B (`wp-notes/halo-t1.md`). Steps: the spike's port as it was +477 / +478; the stage bound to the manager through `pipe()` +383 / +376; the touch navigation moved into the viewport +383 / +420; virtual modifiers and the display overlay, trimmed +722–724 / +751–809. What is eager: the stage slot and `pipe()`, `navigate` / `endNavigate`, the tool-event fields (`pressure`, `pointerId`, `coalesced`, `virtual`), `setModifierSource` and `modifierSource`, `setDisplayOverlay` with the per-pane x-ray opacity. Lazy: the arbiter's state machine, the tolerance table and switch, `createModifierSource`, the two-finger stage, the curve editors' per-pointer tables | A per-pointer table in Pro's eager Graph Editor cost +482 B (measured, then moved out: ST-T1-4); the manipulator's touch scale (ST-T1-5) does not fit | **owner** (OQ-3), T12 |
| ST-T1-1 | How the arbiter reaches the pipeline (A12) | The manager binds the shell's stage to **itself** (`ArbiterHost`: `paneRect`, `pick`, `paneState`, `navigate`, `endNavigate` and one internal `pipe(paneId, e, step, coalesced?)`); the port the state machine uses is built in `input/arbiter.ts` (`hostPort`), not in the manager. −94 B on `/editor` against the spike's port object | A port object in the manager costs every route | T1 |
| ST-T1-2 | `navigate` gestures (A13) | `navigate()` opens a gesture that **`endNavigate()` commits once** (a shot camera's preview, off the undo stack) whatever the pauses; the wheel keeps its 120 ms idle commit. A pair ends its navigation **before** `onTwoFingerTap`, so the undo never meets an open camera preview; `cancel()` ends it too | The spike's wheel burst committed a pinch twice after a 120 ms pause | T1 |
| ST-T1-3 | Taps the tool would pair (C17, A14) | A double-tap is any press within the **second** press's multi-tap window and radius of the previous tap (any pointer); **taps after a double-tap within the window are absorbed** silently; a press the tool would pair with the last click it was handed is a double-tap too. The tool's own double-click (Model select runtime) uses the same per-pointer table (`POINTER_TOLERANCE`: touch 450 ms / 24 px, pen 350 / 8, **mouse 500 / 4**, Pro's `DOUBLE_CLICK_*`), so it never forms a double-click from what it is handed (randomised unit test). The mouse window is **500 ms, not §5.4's 400 ms**, so a Halo double-click and Pro's agree; §5.4's table should say 500 (spec owner) | 400 ms for the mouse lets the tool pair clicks 400–500 ms apart that the arbiter passed through | T1, spec |
| ST-T1-4 | Who switches the tolerances (A15) | `PICK` (theme) holds the tolerances **of the pointer in use** and every consumer reads it at call time; the **arbiter stage switches it per event** (`setPointerTolerance`) and restores the mouse's when cancelled or removed. Studio Pro installs no stage, so Pro on a touch screen keeps the mouse's values (§5.4's "Pro benefits too" would cost eager bytes; deferred). Auto-Rig's two-finger stage does not switch them (AS-ST-15: unchanged). The extrude cap grows its handle tolerances by the manipulator radius' growth; the Quick Rig markers never pick below the pointer's radius; the Model select runtime passes a finger's or the pen's radius to the component picker (`PickOptions.radiusPx`, no `ComponentPicker` change needed). Curve editors: the **Dope Sheet's controller** (lazy) switches its glyph / region / slop per press and move (`setDopeSheetPointer`); the **Graph Editor is in `/editor`'s eager scripts**, so its hit radii switch through `setGraphPointer`, for T10's adapter (measured: +482 B when the eager controller did it) | Switching in the manager covers Pro on touch but costs every route | T1, T10 |
| ST-T1-5 | Manipulator size by pointer type (not built) | **Deferred**: §5.4's render-time scale with hysteresis needs a size factor where the manager configures the kit (`ViewportManager.refreshManipulator`) or inside `ManipulatorKit` (not in T1's files), about 30–40 B eager, which T1's 768 B no longer holds. Proposal: the arbiter stage counts presses by pointer class (two in a row of the other class switch) and sets the factor through one manager setter; `tools.manipulatorSize` is never written | Building it in T1 breaks the 768 B share | **owner** (OQ-3), T6a |
| ST-T1-6 | Auto-Rig's touch navigation after the move | `installSimpleNavigation` and its claims moved to `viewport/src/navigation/touch/` (`BRUSH_TOOLS` with Paint Colour, `MESH_TOOLS` adds the Quick Rig markers); the two-finger handler is a **stage in the same slot as the arbiter** (`createTwoFingerStage`, lazy): no document listeners, no synthetic events, panes by DOM containment, one stage at a time. It loads where `maxTouchPoints > 0`, as before (Playwright's WebKit reports 0, and has no CDP for two fingers anyway). `autorig/src/nav/**` re-exports | Loading it on coarse pointers too costs `/autorig` bytes for an untestable case | T1 |
| ST-T1-7 | Virtual modifiers and the display overlay (A16) | `ModifierSource` is `get()` + **`attach(deliver)`**: the manager hands the source the active tool's key path and the source sends its held codes' downs and ups (`createModifierSource` keeps the diff; to hand a latched hold to a tool activated later, set it off and on). Tool pointer events merge Shift / Mod / Alt and the stand-in button and carry `virtual: true` while any is on; the rig runtime's held keys read `modifierSource`. `DisplayOverlay` is `Partial<DisplayFlags> & { xrayOpacity? }` (`DisplayFlags` has `shading`); the pane's own display, its memory and its state never change. The **x-ray opacity reaches the display model's own materials** (SceneSync reads theme `XRAY_OPACITY`, now the value in force); the shading materials (`manager/shading.ts` `XRAY_ALPHA`) and mesh-view's x-ray (`MeshPaneDisplay`) need one line each (patches for T6a / T9) | A manager-side held-key diff costs every route | T1, T6a, T9 |
| ST-T1-8 | Chrome inside a pane; the press policy | A pointer-down whose target is `[data-ekza-chrome]` inside the pane element is taken and dropped by the arbiter (C6; chrome outside the pane never reaches it). A buffered press puts `user-select: none` and `-webkit-touch-callout: none` on its pane (every pane already has `touch-action: none`), so a still press is the arbiter's long-press (T1a risk 8; the shell-wide §4.1 policy stays T4 / T6a's) | — | T1, T6a |
| ST-T1-9 | Quick pinch, contact-size palms, `gesturechange` | Built in the arbiter, **off unless configured**: `quickPinch {ms: 300, scale: 0.7}` + `onQuickPinch` (C5, frame all; Settings › Input can leave it out) and `palmContactPx: 30` once a pen was seen (D14; no reported size, no rule). No `GestureEvent` handler exists, so C15 is the shell's: ignore `gesturechange` while `stage.touches() ≥ 2` | — | T6a |
| ST-T1-10 | Lines outside the T1 row | `editor-core/src/types.ts` (T2): `ToolPointerEvent.pressure`, `pointerId`, `coalesced`, `virtual`, `ToolKeyEvent.virtual` (additive). `viewport/src/index.ts`: the arbiter, tolerance, modifier and touch-navigation exports (T0 adds `applyTextureCap` to the same barrel). Not changed (moot under the arbiter, which delivers drags after its slop): `transformTool.ts` and `manipulators/scale/tool.ts` `DRAG_THRESHOLD_PX`, the time slider's tolerances | The integration agent may move them | T2, T12 |
| ST-T1-11 | WebKit run of the Auto-Rig family | On Playwright's WebKit as an iPad Pro 11 (installed build `webkit-2370`): `/autorig` boots, a tapped sample loads, ② renders; `/editor` boots; `ar-00` 3/3; `ar-02` 7/8 — the keyboard test fails because WebKit keeps links out of the Tab order by default (not T1's; T0 / T12 decide whether `webkit-ipad` runs it). T0's `webkit-ipad` project matches `ha-*` only; adding `ar-*` there is a line in T0's `playwright.config.ts` | — | T0, T12 |

### P · T5c — models subpath and progress move (2026-10-24)

Branch `halo/t5c-models`; notes and the per-group measurements in [`wp-notes/halo-t5c.md`](wp-notes/halo-t5c.md).

| ID | Decision | Default chosen | Impact if changed | Decide by |
