With a lazy Halo importer of every name of the four subpaths (probe, not committed): **`/editor` 895,660 B (+538 B)**, `/autorig` +0 B. The input settings (`halo.input`: routing, Halo opens on, Reduce gestures, quick pinch, Thumb key, handedness) are a per-device localStorage record, never Pro's prefs | The pointer kind of each press decides instead of a device guess (S15) | T6b (Settings) |

| ST-T6B-8 | The agents' explicit-target operators | `key.write`, `key.delete`: `searchOnly`; `key.adjust`, `key.retime`, `pose.setAt`, `pose.goals`, `pose.transfer`, `clip.makeLoop`: `wraps` of T5a's wrappers | A Halo surface for explicit writes would duplicate Key ◆ and the Thread | — |

A manifest test checks both against a registry with every chunk | — | T7–T10 |

A rule test walks the static imports from the package entry and fails if the manifest, the parity check, More, a sheet, the input chunk or search become eager. No Halo module is in `/editor` or `/autorig` | A static `moreFor` import put the manifest (+10.8 kB) in `/app`'s initial scripts | T12 (`routes.app` baseline) |

### P · T7 — the Animate stage (2026-10-24)

Build decisions of work package T7 ([wp-note](wp-notes/halo-t7.md)): the Thread, Keys, the Animations row, the
Library, Rec and Reach.

| ID | Decision | Default chosen | Alternative / note | Decide by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ST-T7-1 | What Rec lays over the preferences | Rec on = `{autoKey: true, autoKeyMode: 'all'}`, Rec off = `{autoKey: false}`, as the `shell` layer (`overridePrefs`), only while an animation is active; removed when the shell unmounts. Mode *all*: a new animation's first pose keys (Pro's default *keyed-only* would leave it unkeyed) | Rec only toggling `autoKey` would make J3's first drag an unkeyed tweak | OQ-4 |
| ST-T7-2 | Animate's tray | The docked Halo of a Thread noun, the Animations row and the Thread. The row holds at most three animation targets: up to three chips, or the active one, the most recent other and one `+n` chip (its list holds every animation); then `+ New`, the Blend chip (between keys) and `Library ▸`. One tap on a chip makes it active (the Thread shows its range) and docks its Halo. Empty space's other verbs (Key all, All controls, Reset pose) and its ⋯ More are its long-press ring. At rest ≤ **22** visible targets (Seed-san, iPad landscape) with one animation (the Path's Next chip shown) and with three or more (the Next chip hidden, ST-T7-18) | §4.8's example row adds up to 24 with the Next chip; a row ⋯ goes over 22 | — |
| ST-T7-3 | *+ New* | A card: a name field (empty = the next free "Animation n"), the lengths **30 · 60 · 100 f** (60 the default; 1 · 2 · 4 s with Settings › Time on Seconds) and *Other…* (the numpad, whole frames); a length makes it at the project's fps (`clip.new {character, name, range: {0, frames}}`), so name + length is two touches. A bound game target's open slots show as chips (`target.slot.create {from: 'blank'}`) | The generic `clip.new` form asks for frames without presets | — |
| ST-T7-4 | How Reach takes a drag | A press on the lead selected control (its shape or the mesh it drives, not the manipulator) that is a limb's FK end (hand, foot) or its IK goal, when the limb is IK at the playhead, travels past the slop → Reach cancels the arbiter's buffered press (`pointercancel` to the pane) and keeps the gesture; on lift `pose.reach {position, limb, clip}` once (one step, keyed by Rec). The goal moves on the camera-facing plane through the hand. The preview is a target ring (`solveReach` is not exported, P-T7-1). An FK limb keeps its own drag (the wrapper would switch it to IK over a band only); head and hips are not reachable (P-T5A-5) | A Halo viewport tool wrapping rotate / move would change the session's tool id Pro reads; the pointer layer slot is the rig runtime's | owner (FK Reach) |
| ST-T7-5 | The fitting manipulator (§10.1) | In Animate, the lead selected control gets `rotate` (it turns) or `move` (an IK goal, a pole, the root); only `select`, `move` and `rotate` are replaced; run after the operator that changed the selection | — | — |
| ST-T7-6 | A tap on a turn ring over another body part | Selects that body part (the nearest control under the ring that is not selected); a tap on the selected one stays the grammar's (it reopens the Halo) | P-T7-7 for the grammar | T6a |
| ST-T7-7 | The Thread's edits | Each gesture is one operator run on lift: a dot retime is `select.keys` + `graph.keys.move` (the dot is drawn moved while dragging, nothing is previewed in the document); a band is `time.selectBand` while dragging, its body `graph.keys.move` (Thumb *Copy*: `graph.keys.copyTo`), its ends `graph.keys.scale` | Pro's `KeyDrag` previews in the document; Halo's sources make no store writes (§20.1) | — |
| ST-T7-8 | The band-source seam (§20.3 item 18) | `provideBandSource(app, source)` in curve-editor: the first source registers `time.selectBand` and the `timeline.context.*` items unless an owner already did; the focused source redraws; the last one removes what it registered. The Thread provides it from a lazy chunk when it mounts (the curve editor never loads with `/app`'s first scripts, ha-12) | Pro's slider host keeps its own registration (P-T7-5) | T12 |
| ST-T7-9 | The Keys layer | Pro's `DopeSheetController` behind `attachDopeSheetTouch`; the controller's 20 px rows shown 1.8× (36 pt); a long-press then a drag is a band, a long-press alone docks the key's (or the playhead's) Halo, two fingers pan and zoom, a double-tap runs `dopeSheet.frame`; the Dope Sheet contributions are registered while the layer is open unless Pro's are | New row geometry in the controller would change Pro | — |
| ST-T7-10 | The Thread's docked Halos | The verb table's petals of `keyDot`, `band`, `playhead` and `clip` in slot order; a ▾ petal opens its choices (one touch each); a band's *Move* and *Stretch* offer ‹ › one frame and ×0.5 / ×2 (the drags do the rest); ⋯ More opens the frame's More over that noun (`sheetNoun`); an animation's *Settings* open in its Halo (ST-T7-20). One Halo at a time: a docked noun closes the canvas Halo and the other way round | — | — |
| ST-T7-11 | The Library's calls | *Add* = `library.useClip {character, clip}` (Pro's Clip Library call; `params` only when not the identity); *Add starter set* = `autorig.clip.add` per clip (Auto-Rig ④, slots from the pack); *Arms out* = 15°; the preview stops when the sheet closes | Auto-Rig's `autorig.clip.add` for every Add would leave `library.useClip` without a Simple-layout surface | — |
| ST-T7-12 | The stage's words | `i18n/animate.ts` (and `i18n/channels.ts`), linted by `animate-words.test.ts` with the shell's rules (the Library's walking kind is *Walk & run*, never *Moves*) | P-T7-3 folds them into the shell's lint | T6-INT |
| ST-T7-13 | What the touch counter counts in Animate | Every tap on the stage's chrome, a filled text field once (its tap), a tool's drag on the canvas on its manipulator or a selected thing, a Reach drag, a ruler scrub or band, a chip scrub | The grammar counts its own taps, long-presses and petals | — |
| ST-T7-14 | The shell hooks | `controller.ts`: `startAnimate(controller)` in `start()` and the `animate` test-API slice; `Frame.tsx`: `useAnimateDock` (the dock in place of `Tray` in Animate, its measured lift for the insets, `hideNext` around `FrameNext`); `Sheets.tsx`: the `halo.time` slot renders the lazy `thread/TimeSettings`; Settings' model gives the Animation section that slot — seams that merged with T6-INT without conflicts | — | T12 |
| ST-T7-15 | AS-ST-5 for J3 and J5 | A Node test in the Halo package on the production kernel: Halo's calls built by its own modules vs Pro's (and Auto-Rig ④'s) panel calls, compared as exported VRMA bytes; the pose drags (the same viewport tool in both shells) as the same `channel.setValues` edits | — | — |
| ST-T7-16 | The Blend chip | ⟨Blend 50 %⟩ between two keys of the selection; lifting runs `key.tween {mode: 'tween', amount: (v − 50) · 2, target: 'frame'}` once; −25 … 125 % overshoots; no live preview | A preview would need a session pose source | — |
| ST-T7-17 | Curves before T10 | Layer 2 shows a stand-in with *Open in the Pro layout* | T10 replaces it (P-T7-4) | T10 |
| ST-T7-18 | The Next chip in Animate | Hidden while the Animations row shows two or more chips (`useAnimateDock().hideNext`); the Path's readiness glyph still says what is next. The visible-target counter counts the chip and its ✕ as two targets, as for every stage (ST-37 unchanged) | Counting the ✕ with its chip would weaken ST-37 everywhere; a row ⋯ or fewer chips would hide the owner's priority | coordinator 2026-10-24 |
| ST-T7-19 | Open Settings and the 36-target budget | An animation's open Settings take its Halo's verb row: ← back to the verbs, *Settings · name*, ✕. The fps rule is one *Keep frames* toggle. With Settings open the iPad landscape count is 36 | The verbs beside the Settings counted 41 | — |
| ST-T7-20 | An animation's Settings (§10.6) | In its docked Halo: fps 24 · 30 · 60 (+ the clip's own) with *Keep frames*; start, end and length as time chips (scrub, or tap for the numpad; whole frames); *Loops*; travel *With the hips · In place*; the contact frame *At the playhead* / *Clear*; tags. Every change is one `clip.setSettings` run (one undo step), Pro's clip settings call | Numbers' clip section (its Pro-named fields) stays under ⋯ More | — |
| ST-T7-UNIT | Time unit | **Owner 2026-10-24: frames by default** ("cycles are usually 50, at most 100 frames; animating in frames is easier"). Settings › Time *Frames · Seconds* (Animation section, the `halo.time` slot) is a per-device Halo preference in localStorage (`ekza-studio:halo:time:v1`); a stored choice is kept. It sets the Thread's readout and ruler (whole-frame labels, step by zoom: 0 · 10 · 20 … or 0 · 12 · 24 …; beat ticks unchanged), the docked Halos' titles, an animation's Settings and the Curves axis (T10). J3 stays a two-second wave, made with the 60 f chip | Seconds by default (v0.2's §10.3) | owner |

## Q. Character contract, defaults chosen (2026-10-24)

Defaults and owner questions of the character contract between Ekza Studio, Ekza Hub and OMOBA
([CHARACTER-CONTRACT-SPEC](CHARACTER-CONTRACT-SPEC.md) v0.1, [ADR 0024](adr/0024-character-contract.md), Proposed).
Section P (the touch shell, `ST-*`) lives on `design/studio-touch` until that spec merges.

- Every row has a default, so that work can start; the owner may override any of them.
- Rows `CC-Q*` are the spec's open questions (§13). The default stands until the owner answers.
- Build-time decisions are added by the work packages as `CC-C<n>-*` rows; C7 consolidates them as `CC-INT-*`.

| ID | Question | Default chosen | Impact if changed | Decide by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CC-1 | Who owns the description of what a hero needs? | **OMOBA**: `omoba.character-contract.v1`, generated from `skills.skillfx`, the hero and skill catalogues and the motion library, committed as `shared/assets/catalog/character-contract.json`; `revision` = `sha256-` of canonical JSON (the `omoba.asset-catalog.v1` rule); licence CC0-1.0 | Studio- or Hub-owned lists drift from the game | C0 freeze |
| CC-2 | Slot keys | **Gameplay identity**: `base.<state>`, `basic.<class>.<n>` (`basic.wildspark.rockets.0`), `skill.<skillId>.release\|recast\|windup`; stable, never reused | Key-based ids break borrowed skills and the lab | C0 freeze |
| CC-3 | What is custom-able in v1? | **101 of 107 slots**: `base.idle`, `base.run`, `base.death`, every basic attack, every skill release and recast. Wind-ups (5) in v1.1 (`CC-Q2`); `base.walk` reserved; Studio vocabulary slots without an engine state (`attack`, `cast`, `hit`, `jump`, `dance`, …) ignored by OMOBA and left out of the package | Adding wind-ups needs telegraph-fitting rules for custom clips | C0 freeze |
| CC-4 | Ship defaults in the package? | **No.** The slot map lists every custom-able slot of the target view as `custom` or `default`; `default` plays OMOBA's library motion with its row | Shipping defaults adds bytes and freezes OMOBA's tuning into packages | C0 freeze |
| CC-5 | How does a custom clip play? | **As authored**: rate 1, start 0; duration, loop and contact from the clip; Studio shows the default's effective timing as the target and offers *Fit timing* | Declared playback per slot (`CC-Q11`) adds a rule surface | C3 |
| CC-6 | Package format | **One VRM 1.0** (0.x accepted) with one animation per custom clip named by its primary slot id, slots may share an animation, plus `asset.extras.ekza_animation_set_v1`; a dev zip (VRM turned to −Z, per-slot VRMA, manifest) for local tests | A separate pack asset needs a protocol bump; a zip does not fit Hub's one source per revision | C0 freeze |
| CC-7 | Rule numbers v1 | Contact `(contact − start·duration)/rate ≤ 0.15 s`; action window 0.15–1.2 s; run 0.4–1.2 s; idle 1–4 s; death 0.5–3 s; hips drift ≤ 0.15 leg length (death 0.75), vertical ≤ 0.6; loop closure 2° / 0.01 leg length; ≤ 4096 keys per channel, ≤ 60 Hz; ≤ 48 custom slots, ≤ 4 MiB animation data; rotation on driven bones only, translation on hips only, no scale or morph channels. **Tuned in C0** against all 57 shipped clips | Tighter caps reject more creator work; looser caps risk readability | C0 freeze |
| CC-8 | How are Studio, the passport and Hub kept in step? | Rule ids and parameters in the contract; **TypeScript (Studio) and Rust (passport, also run by the builder)**; Hub checks the structural `pack.*` subset only; OMOBA owns conformance fixtures run by all three; unknown rule ids fail closed; no passport-as-WASM in Studio (MPL-2.0 in MIT, bundle) | A third implementation (Python) would drift | C0 freeze |
| CC-9 | Verdicts | **Strict at the gates** (Studio export error, builder rejects with a per-slot report), **lenient at runtime** (a failing slot plays its default, logged once) | Runtime rejection of the whole avatar would punish revision drift | C3 |
| CC-10 | Is a character tied to its hero? | **No**: `base.*` on any class, `basic.<class>.*` on that class, `skill.<id>.*` whenever the avatar casts that skill; the target hero is a label ("Made for Wildspark") and the default pick when equipping (`CC-Q1` for collab locks) | A lock needs catalogue flags, equip UI and admission checks | C3 |
| CC-11 | Contract hosting | **Hub registry**: `project_contracts` + `project_contract_channels` (test, live), public CORS `*` immutable routes, owner routes, operator CLI publish for the MVP; per-hero requirements `character.<hero>` derived on publish | Static hosting only would leave builder, Studio and review without one pinned revision | C4 |
| CC-12 | Hub profile and builder | `desktop/humanoid-glb-v2` with builder `omoba-humanoid-glb-v2` (keeps custom clips, no five generic clips, Rust `validate-motion-pack` inside, per-slot report); **the v1 rendition is still built** for old clients (`CC-Q4`) | Without v1, old clients lose the character | C4 |
| CC-13 | Staged release | Release pointers per **channel test → live**; OMOBA's owner approves into test, promotes to live; clients choose by `OMOBA_ASSET_CHANNEL` (Dev builds and the dev lobby = test) | Without test, the owner reviews motion only from captures | C5 |
| CC-14 | Licence and rights | Licence and attribution **snapshotted per revision**; `revision_rights` (creator, co-author, rights holder; terms hash; consent; withdrawal); an owner decision on a collab revision is refused until every rights holder consented (`CC-Q5`) | Per-asset free text cannot prove consent for a revision | C5 |
| CC-15 | Submit from the editor | **Hub submit page via `postMessage`** (`ekza-studio-submit/v1`): no tokens in the editor, consent on Hub; OAuth + PKCE with a `studio-upload` scope and CORS write routes later (C5b) (`CC-Q7`) | PKCE first: ≈ +9 agent-days and a privacy-rule exception | C5 |
| CC-16 | Where Studio reads contracts | Pinned copy in the document → **bundled same-origin snapshot** (`apps/web/public/contracts/omoba/`) → live Hub fetch only on an explicit tap or on Submit (`CC-Q8`) | Automatic live fetch breaks the same-origin privacy rule (FR-AR-11, NFR-08) | C1 |
| CC-17 | Target binding in the project | Extension table `ekza.targets`, one target per character, contract JSON pinned inline; `SCHEMA_VERSION` 4 unchanged | `CharacterRecord.target` would need schema v5 | C1 |
| CC-18 | Studio export profile | New `ekza/omoba-humanoid-v2` ("OMOBA character (VRM 1.0 with your animations)"), allowed for model characters; `ekza/omoba-humanoid-v1` stays for asset characters | — | C2 |
| CC-19 | Clip metadata round-trip | `EkzaClipMetaV1.label` added, written only when non-null (M1 goldens unchanged); importers restore slot, contact, root motion, tags, label; an imported package offers *Restore the OMOBA target* | A `schema: 2` bump would change every golden | C2 |
| CC-20 | When does Studio change its OMOBA copy and show Submit? | When the bundled index says Hub accepts the profile (`submit: true`); until then "OMOBA plays its own motion library" stays true and Submit is hidden | Early copy would promise what the game does not do | C5 |
| CC-21 | Old clients meeting a v2 slug | C3 verifies they show the default skin; otherwise v2 ships with the next protocol version | — | C3 |
| CC-22 | Studio bundle | Everything lazy (one `contract` chunk, the Target panel, operators behind a prefix check); ≤ 512 B eager per route | — | C1, C2 |
| CC-23 | Ekza Space and character packages | v1 packages are submitted to OMOBA only (Space would skip its idle/walk for a VRM with embedded clips, ADR 0010 §6) | Space support needs a Space change to read the slot map | C5 |
| CC-24 | Studio's local OMOBA test file for targets | The dev zip replaces the kit for bound characters and turns VRM 1.0 to rest facing −Z as OMOBA's tools do (fixes the likely backwards kit, P-4) | — | C2 |
| CC-Q1 | **owner** — Lock collab characters to their hero? | No lock; badge and default pick only | A lock is a catalogue flag + equip UI + admission | C3 |
| CC-Q2 | **owner** — Custom wind-ups in v1? | v1.1 (5 skills: dawn_ray, horizon_wave, furnace_breath, orbital_collapse, mirror_guard) | +≈ 4 agent-days across C2/C3 | C0 freeze |
| CC-Q3 | **owner** — Contract licence, default preview pack | Contract CC0-1.0; the optional default preview pack (default motions as VRMA on a CC0 rig, from CC0-derived data) is published CC0 | Without it, Studio previews only the 14 shared starter clips | C0 |
| CC-Q4 | **owner** — Keep building v1 renditions? | Yes, for clients without v2 | Old clients show no character | C4 |
| CC-Q5 | **owner** — Rights-holder countersign before test or before live? | Before any owner approval (test included) | Test-first lets the rights holder see it in the game before signing | C5 |
| CC-Q6 | **owner** — One combined curator + owner step for OMOBA characters? | Two gates as today | Faster review; one person sees both moderation and fit | C5 |
| CC-Q7 | **owner** — Approve the Hub submit page now, PKCE later? | Yes | — | C5 |
| CC-Q8 | **owner** — Allow a user-initiated live contract fetch (cross-origin GET, no credentials) as an exception to the same-origin rule? | Yes, only on *Check for newer* and *Submit* | Without it, a new revision reaches users with the next Studio release | C1 |
| CC-Q9 | **owner** — Paid collab characters? | Free approved first; paid later through the existing commerce consent and ticket path | — | after C7 |
| CC-Q10 | **owner** — One package covering several heroes' kits? | v1.1 | — | after C7 |
| CC-Q11 | **owner** — Let creators declare playback rate/start per slot? | No (play as authored) | One more rule surface | C3 |
| CC-Q12 | **owner** — Final hosted origins | Editor `https://studio.ekza.io`, Hub `https://hub.ekza.io`; dev: Hub `hub.dev.ekza.io`, editor dev origin to be named (also answers `M-AI-INT-9`) | Allow-lists and links follow | C5, C7 |
| CC-Q13 | **owner** — Withdrawal and takedown | A withdrawn consent or a takedown removes the release pointers; clients drop the character at the next catalogue refresh; no claim on copies already shown | Legal wording | C5 |
| CC-Q14 | **owner** — A skill removed from the roster | Its slot ids are retired, never reused; packages that filled them keep working for the remaining slots | — | C0 freeze |

### Q.1 Build decisions of C1 (Studio reads the contract; 2026-10-24)

Branch `feat/character-contract-studio`; notes in [wp-notes/cc-c1.md](wp-notes/cc-c1.md). C1 adopted the Ekza Game
Protocol's names before C0 freezes (`GP-21`, GAME-PROTOCOL-SPEC §12.3): the document is `ekza.game-contract.v1` with the
profile `omoba.character.v1`; where a row below names a v0.1 term, section R's row wins.

| ID | Question | Default chosen | Impact if changed | Decide by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CC-C1-1 | Layering of the Studio side | **The protocol apart from the profile** (GAME-PROTOCOL-SPEC §3, §12.3): io `contract/model.ts` is the protocol model (game, rigs, sockets, packaging, rules, slots of kind `animation`, `asset` or `socket` with `AnimationSlotSpec` markers and `leadInSecs` and an `AssetSlotSpec`, families, targets with sections and offers); `gameContract.ts` is the one strict reader of `ekza.game-contract.v1` (minors, strictness, cross-references, rules, profiles, revision); `profiles/omobaCharacterV1.ts` checks `ext["omoba.character.v1"]` strictly (document, target, animation slot, asset slot). The status function is generic over slot kinds: asset and socket slots have no filler before GP6 and show their default or `fixed` | — | GP0 (extraction into `@ekza/gp`) |
| CC-C1-2 | Target record (§8.2 sketch) | `{id: 'tgt:<characterId>', character, contract: {schema: 'ekza.game-contract.v1', game, revision}, document, target, aliases, hub, deleted}` (§12.3: `hero` → `target`); the derived id makes one target per character structural (a rebind restores and patches the record) | — | C2 |
| CC-C1-3 | Contract data until C0 publishes | The converter prototype's `game-contract.full.json` (113 slots: 107 animation, 6 Wildspark asset slots; 18 targets; revision `sha256-68441bd3…`, recomputed by Studio and by OMOBA's Python `canonical`), committed as `fixtures/contracts/omoba.game-contract.json` and the bundled live revision, with the protocol, profile and package schemas. Swap with `node tooling/sync-contract.mjs --from <omoba-bevy>/shared/assets/catalog/game-contract.json --schema …` once C0 lands (C0 emits animation slots only; GP5 adds the asset slots) | Fixture expectations that name slots or counts follow the official data | C0 merge |
| CC-C1-4 | Contract sources | As CC-16: pinned in the document → session cache → bundled same-origin snapshot (size and SHA-256 against its index, revision recomputed). The live Hub fetch (`CC-Q8`) and Cache Storage are not built (Hub has no contract routes before C4); `EditorServices.contractsPath` (default `/contracts/`) instead of a resolver member | *Check for newer* arrives with C4/C5 | C5 |
| CC-C1-5 | Bundled index | `ekza.contract-index.v1` as GAME-PROTOCOL-SPEC §8.10: `{schema, game, contractSchema: 'ekza.game-contract.v1', profiles, channels: {live, test}, submit, revisions: [{revision, file, bytes, sha256, previousRevision, targets, slots}]}` under `apps/web/public/contracts/<game>/`; `profiles` is the sorted union of the listed revisions' `profiles.used`. Written and checked by `tooling/sync-contract.mjs` (`--check` in `pnpm run test:tooling`) | — | C5 |
| CC-C1-6 | Schema check of the bundled snapshot in the local gate | No JSON Schema library (no new dependency): the strict reader runs on the snapshot, and an io test asserts the reader's key sets equal the committed `ekza.game-contract.v1` schema's `properties`/`required` per object, so code and Appendix A cannot drift apart; the profile schema is mirrored by the profile module's strict checks | A real schema validator would need a dev dependency (integration decision) | GP0 |
| CC-C1-7 | Open vocabulary mechanism | A slot id with a dot is a contract slot (v1 names have none). An eager 1-kB module asks the lazy target chunk; `clip.setSettings` loads the chunk in `prepare` when given one and refuses a slot outside the bound view, a slot not open in this contract, or a second holder (one clip per contract slot per character); `target.slot.assign` and `library.useClip {slot}` move the slot instead | — | C2 |
| CC-C1-8 | Filling a slot from the library in one step | `library.useClip` and `autorig.clip.add` take an optional `slot` (in-place parameters for in-place slots): one *Use Clip* step. The spec's `target.slot.create` composition (blank / default / file, contract settings) stays in C2 | — | C2 |
| CC-C1-9 | Document checks and Validation rows in C1 | Checks read the contract's numbers: `clip.duration` (window), `clip.loop-closure` (the loop flag), `clip.keys` (frame rate ≤ `maxSampleRateHz`), `clip.markers` (the `contact` marker on the clip's contact frame, present when required and within `maxSecs` + `toleranceSecs`; other named markers wait for C2's timeline markers and show a warning when required), `clip.in-place` (root motion; a **warning**: drift is measured on the bytes, C2), `clip.min-interval` (warning); `slot.not-open` for a fill of a fixed slot; unknown namespaced rules (`omoba.asset.grip`) are listed as *checked by the game when you submit* and never block. Validation rows of area `animation` are **advisory in C1** (errors show as warnings, warnings as info), so nothing blocks a plain VRM export; C2's package export makes errors block | Blocking in C1 would stop plain exports of bound characters | C2 |
| CC-C1-10 | Auto-Rig ④ contract mode (pulled forward from C6) | *For a game…* → target (2 taps, loads the chunk and the contract on that tap, not on entry); bound, the checklist of the target's `character` offer replaces the free clip list; per row *Game default ▶* (where the starter pack has the motion), *Pick from library…*, *Use clip…*, *Use default*; extras *Map to a slot…*; the step's OMOBA note hides in contract mode only. Not built: per-row `.vrma` drop, *Animate in Studio ▸*, ⑤'s package target | — | C6 |
| CC-C1-11 | Pro Target panel | An extra panel docked left as a tab beside Clips (`EXTRA_PANEL_SLOTS`, no default-layout change), opened from Windows › Target (the *Target ▾* entry in the Clips header of §8.4 is not built: it cost eager bytes, `CC-C1-17`). It shows the target's whole kit (GAME-PROTOCOL-SPEC §6.1: clips and objects; object rows dimmed with their default or *made by the game*), one summary per offer and the deferred rules. A/B = *▶ Game default* (the starter-pack clip whose id is the default's name with `_`→`-`) and the row name activating the custom clip. Timeline markers, contract-locked Clip Settings fields and the package export are C2; Clip Settings keeps an unchanged target slot and shows a slot outside vocabulary v1 by its id | — | C2 |
| CC-C1-12 | Deep link `?target=<game>/<character>&rev=` (§8.1 item 4) | **Deferred** to C5/C6: it changes the editor and Auto-Rig hosts (the Halo work packages' area) and serves Hub's hero pages, which do not exist yet | — | C5 |
| CC-C1-13 | When `ekza.targets` registers | When the target chunk loads (first `target.*` run, the Target panel, Auto-Rig ④'s target line). Before that the rows are opaque in this build too; the memory store opens every partition, so nothing reads differently. Auto-Rig ④ reads `get('ekza.targets', 'tgt:<id>')` to decide whether to load the chunk | An eager registration costs first-visit bytes | C2 |
| CC-C1-14 | P-4 (C-pre) | **Fixed in the existing kit** ahead of C2's dev zip: `packOmobaKit` stages the VRM as OMOBA's `convert_vrm_to_glb.py` does (`stageVrmForOmoba`, a port of `normalize_vrm1_rest_facing`: a +Z VRM 1.0 gets its roots turned 180° about Y and `asset.extras.omoba_rest_yaw180`; VRM 0.x byte for byte). Verified on `omoba-bevy@dev`: the client never turns a model at runtime, so the old kit's VRM 1.0 faced backwards | — | C7 (in-game check) |
| CC-C1-15 | P-5, P-6 (C-pre) | P-5: `ekza/omoba-humanoid-v1` keeps the five clips its v1 passport requires; its label and FEATURES row now say "legacy passport clips" (the v2 profile is C2). P-6: the copy stays until C3 ships (`CC-20`) | — | C3 |
| CC-C1-16 | Agents and the target operators | `target.*` carry `agent: denied` until C6 adds `target_describe` and friends (the policy test needs every operator classified) | — | C6 |
| CC-C1-17 | Eager bytes (≤ 512 B per route, CC-22) | Measured on the production build: `/editor` 892,300 B (+510 over 891,790), `/autorig` 653,008 B (+480 over 652,528). What stays eager: the four-export `workflows/targetSlots.ts` (dotted-id test, the hooks the chunk installs, the chunk loader), the `clip.setSettings` poll/prepare lines, one `lazyOperatorChunk` branch, the panel registration and its dock slot, the Clip Settings check. The Target panel body rides the Clip Library's lazy group (one chunk loader for both); the target chunk is its own boundary (`character-target`, 16.3 kB) | A Clips-header entry or a separate panel chunk would cost ≈ 35–70 B each | C2 |

### Q.2 Build decisions of C2 (package, lints, timeline markers; 2026-10-24)

Branch `feat/character-contract-package`; notes in [wp-notes/cc-c2.md](wp-notes/cc-c2.md). Names are the Ekza Game
Protocol's (`GP-21`): the manifest is `ekza_package_v1` (`ekza.package.v1`), the rules are the core vocabulary.

| ID | Question | Default chosen | Impact if changed | Decide by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CC-C2-1 | Where the byte rules live and how they report | io `contract/packageCheck.ts`: `checkCharacterPackage(bytes, {contract, retired})` → per package and per slot issues and measured values, `gateVerdict` (the conformance fixtures' shape), `validationReport` (`ekza.validation-report.v1`, stage `tool`). Parameters only from the contract (rule params, slot window and markers, rig, packaging); a rule the contract does not list is not checked. Studio runs it on the written package (read-back) and the issues become the export's `package.<rule>` lints; the document checks stay the `contract.<rule>` Validation rows. Two code families because the export merges a profile row over a byte lint of the same code: a document warning must not hide a byte error | One code family would need the merge rule changed for every export | GP0 (extraction into `@ekza/gp`) |
| CC-C2-2 | Order of the package rules | As the gate verdicts of OMOBA's fixtures expect: `pack.manifest`, `pack.contract`, `pack.target` (each stops), `pack.bytes` (stops: "checked before the slots"), then `pack.slot-known`, `pack.ref`, `pack.unreferenced` (warning), `rig.humanoid`; any package error skips the slots (verdict `skipped`) and the gate verdict drops the warnings; then each custom slot alone: `clip.channels`, `clip.sampler`, `clip.keys`, `clip.duration`, `clip.in-place`, `clip.loop-closure`, `clip.markers`, `clip.min-interval` (warning) | — | C3 (the passport's order) |
| CC-C2-3 | Measurements the spec leaves open | Sample rate per channel = (keys − 1) / (last − first time) (the mean rate); first key at `startsAtSecs` ± `timeEpsilonSecs`; unit quaternions within `unitQuatEpsilon` of length 1; duration = the last finite key time; hips drift = the largest horizontal (XZ) offset of the hips translation keys from the hips' rest translation (C0's N1: `reference: "rest-pose"`; `first-key` when a contract says so), through the hips parent's rest world transform, in leg lengths (hips rest height above the mean of the feet; the rig's `referenceM` when unmeasurable); up and down separately on Y against `maxUp` / `maxDown` (a contract without them: `maxVertical` both ways); every cap read from the rule's params; loop seam = the largest angle between a rotation channel's first and last key (2·acos\|dot\|) and the hips offset first → last; markers compared with the clip metadata within `toleranceSecs`; custom clips play at rate 1 from 0 (`customPlayback`) | C3's passport must measure the same way or the shared fixtures disagree | C3 |
| CC-C2-4 | OMOBA's conformance fixtures | C0's (omoba-bevy PR #109 @ `c0fe23f`): all 45 package cases, the 12 reader cases, the two fixture contracts and `manifest.json`, copied by `tooling/sync-contract.mjs --fixtures` into `fixtures/contracts/omoba/v1/` (SHA-256 pinned to the fixture manifest, `--check` in the gate, `.gitattributes -text`). io's test gives every package case its gate verdict (rule ids, warnings, per-slot verdicts and warnings) and every reader case its verdict and reason code, except two package expectations that disagree with §5.1 because the generator declares one rule per case: `clip-too-short` (its manifest keeps contact 0.1 s while the clip's metadata says 0.05 s → also `clip.markers`) and `ok-shared-animation` (the 0.5 s rocket basic attack exceeds `minIntervalSecs` 0.4375 s → warning `clip.min-interval`). Reported to C0; the test records both **Update 2026-10-24:** C0 fixed both disputed expectations (`clip-too-short`, `ok-shared-animation`) in omoba-bevy PR #116 (`699bc85`, fixture manifest `510100c0…`); Studio matches all 45 package cases with no override. | C0 regenerates the two expectations (or the rule text changes) | C0 freeze |
| CC-C2-5 | VRM 1.0 thumbs | The contract's bone lists (the motion library's `drivenBones`) speak VRM 0.x names; a VRM 1.0 humanoid is read through `clip.channels.params.vrm1BoneMap` (C0's N4: `ThumbMetacarpal` → `ThumbProximal`, `ThumbProximal` → `ThumbIntermediate`), as OMOBA's retargeter does; a contract without the parameter gets the same built-in map. Found by the headless round trip before C0 published the parameter: Studio's VRM 1.0 writer maps the thumbs | — | C0 freeze |
| CC-C2-6 | Named markers (`release`, `fire`, `footstep_l`, …) | The contact stays `ClipRecord.contact`; the others live in the extension table `ekza.clipMarkers` (`mk:<clipId>`, partition `char:<id>`, registered with the target chunk; `SCHEMA_VERSION` 4 stays). The slot statuses check every marker the slot declares; the manifest writes the declared markers that are set; `EkzaClipMetaV1.markers` carries the named ones. OMOBA's contract declares only `contact` today | A `ClipRecord.markers` field would need schema v5 | C6 |
| CC-C2-7 | Clip metadata round-trip (§8.14) | `EkzaClipMetaV1` gains `label` and `markers`, written only when set (package clips: `label` = the clip's name; M1 goldens unchanged). Importing a Studio-written file (`ekza-animator` provenance) restores each clip's slot (when no other live clip of the character holds it), contact, root motion, tags, label (as the readable clip name) and named markers (when the markers table is registered). *Restore the OMOBA target* from an imported package's manifest is **not built** | The restore offer needs a UI on import (Auto-Rig ①, Studio's importer) | C6 |
| CC-C2-8 | `target.slot.create` | One transaction over the shipped operators' `prepare`/`apply` (`clip.new`, `library.useClip`, `file.importAnimation`) and the `clip.setSettings` body (the runner has no history-group API). Settings: the slot (moved off other clips, its alias dropped), Loop = the slot's `loop`, root motion in place for `inPlace` slots, the contact at the default's time capped at its limit (`floor`), named markers at their targets; `blank` = 30 fps and the window's target length kept inside the window; `default` = the starter-pack copy of the default motion (`_` → `-`), refused when the pack has none; `file` takes the file's first clip; the clip is named from the slot label ("Shockline W - release"). The Pro panel's *From library* now runs it (C1 ran `library.useClip`), so its undo label is "Use Clip for <slot>". Auto-Rig ④'s checklist is unchanged | — | C6 (Halo recipe) |
| CC-C2-9 | `target.fix` | Fixes per rule: *Make loop* = `clip.makeLoop` matchEnds (`rootTravel` inPlace for in-place slots) and Loop on; *Set in place* = root motion in place; *Fit timing* = the clip's keys, handles, range end, contact and markers scaled about its start to the window's target length (Studio's own scaling: `key.retime` keeps the range and needs a target list); *Set contact* / *Set <marker>* = the target time capped at the limit; *Use default* for a fixed slot. *Fit timing* is also offered next to a contact issue (§9 F2). Validation rows carry the fix (`target.fix {character, slot, rule, marker?}`); Auto-Rig ⑤'s checklist and the Validation panel run it | — | — |
| CC-C2-10 | The package | The custom clips of the target's character offer in the offer's order (first appearance gives the animation index; an alias shares its clip's index; extras stay out), each named by its primary slot; `ekza_package_v1` lists every customizable slot of the offer once; provenance `{tool: 'ekza-studio', toolVersion}`. A slot clip without keys blocks with `package.clip.channels` ("has no keys yet"). The writer keeps every channel it bakes: a channel the game does not drive blocks through `clip.channels` instead of being stripped | Stripping would hide a rig the game cannot play | C3 |
| CC-C2-11 | Export routes | `file.exportCharacter {profile: 'ekza/omoba-humanoid-v2', clips: 'target'}` (one implies the other; the profile defaults its clips to `'target'`); `target.exportPackage {character, devZip?, fileName?}` saves the package or the developer zip (`<slug>-omoba-dev.zip`: the package VRM through `stageVrmForOmoba`, `animations/<slot>.vrma` from the VRMA exporter, `manifest-entry.json`, `slot-map.json` copied from the written VRM, `README.txt`); Pro: the Export dialog's profile *OMOBA character (VRM 1.0 with your animations)* with *Developer zip*, and the Target panel's *Export package* / *Developer zip*; Auto-Rig ⑤: target `omoba-hero`, and `omoba-kit` writes the developer zip for a bound model character (CC-24; ⑤'s footer names `<slug>-omoba-dev.zip` then). The agent kinds `omoba_hero` / `omoba_dev_zip` are C6's | — | C6 |
| CC-C2-12 | Rights in the package | The manifest schema has no rights block (`additionalProperties: false`): rights are declared in the VRM meta (authors, licence → `VRMC_vrm.meta`, `thirdPartyLicenses` from provenance), required as errors by the v2 profile (`vrm.meta-required`, `vrm.licence-unset`, as Space), the clips' `derivedFrom` in `ekza_animation_v1`, and `provenance`; OMOBA's rule table lists no `rights.license`, so Studio checks none. Verified rights stay Hub's (C5) | A `rights.*` rule in the contract would be checked here | C5 |
| CC-C2-13 | Timeline markers | A lazy layer of the time slider (`TimeSliderLayer`, `setLayer`, the controller's `onView` callback): the slider loads it when the active clip holds a contract slot; it draws the declared markers (◆ set, ◇ at the target while unset, dashed limit line), the window bracket (min … target … max, red past max or short of min) and the lead-in band; dragging a ◆ previews and runs `target.marker.set` on release (one step); the canvas carries `data-contract-markers` for tests. Halo's Thread is C6's | — | C6 |
| CC-C2-14 | Clip Settings | For a contract slot, Loop, Root motion and the slot are disabled with one *from contract · Unlock* button (`target.slot.clear`) | — | — |
| CC-C2-15 | Validation rows | Strict (the contract's severities) for the v2 profile only; advisory for every other profile (CC-C1-9 stands): the validation service passes the chosen profile to the target hook | — | — |
| CC-C2-16 | Package fixture for OMOBA (§5.3 item 4) | `fixtures/contracts/omoba-package/polybot-wildspark.vrm` (made against C0's revision `sha256-bae71784…`): Polybot rigged by Auto-Rig for Wildspark with a library run and Shockline; refreshed by the headless test (`EKZA_WRITE_PACKAGE_FIXTURE=1`), checked by io's test with the TypeScript rules; its manifest and a full Wildspark fill's pass OMOBA's own schema validator (`export_game_contract.load_schema`, run once) and, in the headless test, the pinned `ekza.package.v1.schema.json` through Ajv (2020-12; `ajv` 8.20.0 joins headless-tests' dev dependencies, already pinned for agent-tools). `tooling/omoba-validate.mjs` against OMOBA's `validate-motion-pack` waits for C3 | — | C3 |
| CC-C2-17 | Eager bytes (target ≤ +256 B, CC-22) | Measured on the production build before the dev merge: `/editor` 892,661 B (+361 over 892,300; the +256 B target missed by ≈ 105 B), `/autorig` 653,051 B (+43). Eager: the slider's layer hook and view callback, the dynamic import of the layer, the Clip Settings lock, the v2 profile data (one shared rules array); everything else is in the target chunk, the rig chunk, the import path or the dialogs. **Superseded by SIZE-1** (§S): one 5,000,000 B gate on every route, targets and the eager-delta allowance report-only, so the overshoot is no issue and nothing is trimmed for size; the lazy boundaries still apply (the target chunk stays its own) | — | — |
| CC-C2-18 | Developer zip facing | The VRM goes through `stageVrmForOmoba` as is. Studio's writer puts a non-humanoid `root` joint above the hips, so turning the scene roots also turns the animated hips; a VRM whose hips is a scene root (other tools) would keep its animated hips rotation unturned (OMOBA's builder inserts a parent, `rig-hips-scene-root-vrm1`) | — | C3/C4 |
| CC-C2-19 | The bundled contract | OMOBA's C0 snapshot (`sha256-bae71784…`, PR #109 @ `c0fe23f`) synced with `--from` and the three `--schema` files (its revision verifies as is: Studio's canonical JSON agrees; the schemas are byte-identical to C1's copies); the converter prototype's revision (`sha256-68441bd3…`) is removed from the bundle and kept as the test fixture `fixtures/contracts/omoba.game-contract.gp5-draft.json` for asset slots (C0 emits animation slots only), on which C1's pure status tests and one reader test now run. Wildspark's checklist shows nine rows, one offer | A project pinned to the prototype keeps opening from its pinned copy | GP5 |
| CC-C2-20 | Reader reason codes | Every refusal carries the fixtures' reason code (`contractRefusalReason`: `size`, `json`, `schema`, `reader-version`, `revision`, `reference`, `unknown-rule`, `profile`; a profile module's or the cross-check's failure tagged where it is thrown); all 12 reader cases agree. Not built: refusing duplicate JSON keys (`json`; `JSON.parse` keeps the last) — no fixture case covers it | A duplicate-key scan before parsing | GP0 |
| CC-C2-21 | Loop seam and hips drift on the document | Measured on the written package only (the export's `package.clip.loop-closure`, `package.clip.in-place`), not continuously on the document as §8.9 lists: a clip whose hips sway past the cap (the starter pack's Cast, Sword Attack and Jump Start in place) blocks at export with the measured value, and no one-tap fix exists for a real drift | A document-side measure needs the evaluated hips per frame (motion analysis) | C6 |
| CC-C2-22 | Robustness of the byte rules (review) | `checkCharacterPackage` never throws (an unforeseen shape becomes a `pack.manifest` refusal); a channel or sampler that is not an object, or an accessor that claims more bytes than the file, is a `clip.sampler` issue before anything is allocated; the rest-pose walk is iterative; the sample-rate cap has a relative slack of 1e-4 (key times are float32); `clip.channels` reads `scale` / `weights` from its params; each slot's driven bones come from its own rig. `pack.ref`'s `nodeNameEqualsRootName` concerns asset fills, which a character package refuses (`pack.slot-known`); `from: 'file'` slots the file's first clip and leaves the others as extras | — | C3 |

## R. Game protocol, defaults chosen (2026-10-24)

Defaults and owner questions of the Ekza Game Protocol ([GAME-PROTOCOL-SPEC](GAME-PROTOCOL-SPEC.md) v0.1,
[ADR 0025](adr/0025-game-protocol.md), Proposed), which turns the character contract of section Q into a protocol any
game can adopt; the OMOBA contract becomes its profile `omoba.character.v1`
([CHARACTER-CONTRACT-SPEC](CHARACTER-CONTRACT-SPEC.md) v0.2). Where a `GP-*` row renames something a `CC-*` row names
(CC-1 document and file, CC-6 manifest key, CC-8 rule ids), the `GP-*` row wins; the substance of every `CC-*` row
stands.

- Every row has a default, so that work can start; the owner may override any of them.
- Rows `GP-Q*` are the spec's open questions (§15). The default stands until the owner answers.
- Build-time decisions are added by the work packages as `GP-<wp>-*` rows.

| ID | Question | Default chosen | Impact if changed | Decide by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| GP-1 | One protocol for every game? | **Yes**: the Ekza Game Protocol (EGP) 1.0 with five documents — `ekza.game-contract.v1`, `ekza.package.v1`, `ekza.validation-report.v1`, `ekza.event.v1`, `ekza.contract-index.v1` — and per-game **profiles** whose data lives only under `ext` and in namespaced rules | Per-game formats make every tool and Hub learn each game | C0 freeze |
| GP-2 | OMOBA's contract under the protocol | `ekza.game-contract.v1` with profile `omoba.character.v1` (used, not required); `omoba.character-contract.v1` is never published | Keeping the old name makes OMOBA a special case forever | C0 freeze |
| GP-3 | Versioning | Major in the schema id; additive minors in `schemaVersion` (`"1.0"`); `minReaderVersion` for safe forward reading; strict parsing for understood minors; `revision` = `sha256-` of canonical JSON (asset-catalog rule, RFC 8785 subset) | Without minors every addition is a breaking change | C0 freeze |
| GP-4 | Ids | Stable; removed ids go to `retired` and are never reused (Hub refuses); slots keyed by gameplay identity; OMOBA asset slots use the `omoba.asset-catalog.v1` requirement ids | — | C0 freeze |
| GP-5 | Model | game → targets (`character`, `any`, `item`, `vehicle`, `prop`) → slots of kind `animation`, `asset` (handheld, projectile, throwable, deployable, attachment, effect) or `socket`; `variant` and `audio` reserved; **families** for extensible lists (`idPrefix`, `maxFills`) | — | C0 freeze |
| GP-6 | How a target says what it accepts | **Offers** (`accepts[]`): one per package kind, with its slots, `listing` and `mustFill`, each mapped to one Hub requirement id (`character.<hero>`, `wildspark.handheld.set`) | Requirements stay hand-seeded in SQL | C4 |
| GP-7 | Generic timing and placement vocabulary | **Markers** (`contact`, `release`, `fire`, footsteps, `land`) and **anchors** (`muzzle`, `trail`, `grip_off`, `impact`); OMOBA's `contact` becomes `animation.markers.contact` | A single `contact` field cannot express throws and shots | C0 freeze |
| GP-8 | Package manifest | One `ekza.package.v1` for every kind, in `asset.extras.ekza_package_v1` (replaces `ekza_animation_set_v1`, never shipped); fills bind by index (animation, root node) | Per-kind manifests multiply parsers | C2 |
| GP-9 | Monolithic or fragments? | **Both**; one package per review unit and per reuse unit; **bundles** (author as one, submit several, approve atomically). OMOBA 1.0: the character package is one VRM with clips; gear is one `asset-pack` GLB per set (the Wildspark set), later also filling the rocket and the trap | Embedding gear in the body couples body and hero | C5 |
| GP-10 | Runtime composition | Per slot: equipped fragment → character package → default; per-slot fallback; `mustFill` sets all or none | — | C3, GP5 |
| GP-11 | Rule vocabulary | Core ids `pack.*`, `rig.*`, `clip.*`, `asset.*`, `socket.*`, `rights.*` with contract parameters; OMOBA renames `pack.extras` → `pack.manifest`, `pack.animation-ref` → `pack.ref`, `clip.contact` → `clip.markers`, `clip.basic-interval` → `clip.min-interval`; adds `pack.target`, explicit `rig.humanoid`, and (GP5) `asset.*` and `omoba.asset.grip` | — | C0 freeze |
| GP-12 | Game-specific rules | Namespaced (`<game>.<scope>.<name>`) with `checkedBy`; run only in the game's sandboxed builder and its runtime; tools without them show *deferred* and do not block; unknown **core** ids fail closed | Fail-closed on game rules would block every generic tool | C0 freeze |
| GP-13 | Reference implementations | TypeScript `@ekza/gp` (MIT, extracted from Studio) and Rust `ekza-gp` (MIT OR Apache-2.0, used by the SDK and OMOBA's passport for core rules; written fresh from the spec, no relicensing); Python in the registry for the structural subset only | A third full implementation would drift | GP0 |
| GP-14 | Events | CloudEvents 1.0 structured JSON, types `io.ekza.gp.<object>.<verb>`, a transactional outbox, per-project `ekzaseq`, at-least-once, 30-day feed | Polling only keeps third-party games blind | GP3 |
| GP-15 | Delivery | Standard Webhooks (HMAC-SHA256, `webhook-id/-timestamp/-signature`, rotation, retries 1 min … 24 h, SSRF guard) and a cursor pull feed for static games | — | GP3 |
| GP-16 | Commands | HTTP + JSON with `Idempotency-Key` on every state-changing POST (24 h) and RFC 9457 problem details carrying the validation report | — | GP2 |
| GP-17 | Game auth | Project keys: publishable `ekza_pk_…` (test channel read) and secret `ekza_sk_…` with scopes `contract:publish`, `events:read`, `usage:write`, `webhooks:manage`, `admission:read`; hashed, shown once, revocable | User tokens in game CI are fragile | GP3 |
| GP-18 | Offline | Contracts committed and bundled; release snapshots (`ekza.release-snapshot.v1`) pulled by `ekza-gp snapshot pull` and pinned by SHA-256; manifests inside the hashed package | — | GP4 |
| GP-19 | Rights | Per package and per fragment revision (`revision_rights` + `share_bps`); bundle approvable only when every member's rights are complete; derivatives declared in `requires` and licence-checked | — | C5 |
| GP-20 | Second game | Ekza Space as the pilot (GP8): a minimal contract with no profile, consumed with no registry code change | Without a second game the generality is untested | after GP4 |
| GP-21 | Work in flight | C0 and C1 adopt the protocol names before the C0 freeze (GAME-PROTOCOL-SPEC §12.3), about one agent-day each | Renaming after the freeze needs a breaking revision | now |
| GP-Q1 | **owner** — Protocol name and home | "Ekza Game Protocol" (EGP); a new public repository `ekza-game-protocol` for schemas, fixtures and reference code | Keeping it in ekza-studio ties every game to the editor's repo | GP0 |
| GP-Q2 | **owner** — Licences | Schemas, fixtures and spec text CC0-1.0; reference code MIT OR Apache-2.0 | Copyleft would keep games from embedding the validator | GP0 |
| GP-Q3 | **owner** — Asset slots in OMOBA's first contract? | No: C0 ships animation slots; GP5 adds the asset slots as a compatible revision | +≈ 2 agent-days in C0 | C0 freeze |
| GP-Q4 | **owner** — Bundles approved atomically? | Yes (approve, promote and revoke the bundle as one; members stay separately equippable) | Per-member approval lets a body go live without its gear | C5 |
| GP-Q5 | **owner** — Gear equip semantics in OMOBA | Slot-keyed: a gear set applies wherever its slots are used, on any body; sets all or none | Hero-locked gear needs catalogue flags and admission checks | GP5 |
| GP-Q6 | **owner** — Outbound webhooks from the registry | Yes, HTTPS only with an egress guard, after GP3; until then the pull feed | Feed only: third-party games must poll | GP3 |
| GP-Q7 | **owner** — Usage aggregates from games | Yes, opt-in per game, aggregates only (no player, account, device or IP data) | Without them creators never see "works in game" | GP3 |
| GP-Q8 | **owner** — Portable rule modules (WebAssembly) so tools can run game rules | Later | Earlier: a sandboxed runtime in Studio and Hub | after GP8 |
| GP-Q9 | **owner** — Signed release snapshots for mirrors | Later; SHA pins in builds suffice | — | after GP4 |
| GP-Q10 | **owner** — Who may create a Hub project for a third-party game | Curated by Ekza (operator CLI), self-serve later | Self-serve needs abuse controls and billing | GP8 |
| GP-Q11 | **owner** — Register a glTF vendor prefix (`EKZA_`) and move the manifest into an extension | No for 1.0; `asset.extras` | A registered extension is more visible to other tools | 1.x |
| GP-Q12 | **owner** — Store revenue shares now | Yes, `share_bps` per rights row, inactive until the commerce ledger pays out | Adding it later needs a migration and re-consent | C5 |

## S. Owner decisions of 2026-10-24 (bundle size, contract licences, merges)

| ID | Decision | Effect | Replaces |
|---|---|---|---|
| SIZE-1 | **One size gate of 5,000,000 B (gzip -9) on every route** (`/editor`, `/autorig`, `/app`), enforced. The owner: a 1 MB-class limit is meaningless for today's connections | `check:size` fails only above 5,000,000 B or when a lazy boundary breaks (the boundaries stay: they keep each shell's code out of the others). Targets (850,000 / 620,000 / 660,000 B) and the eager-delta allowance are reported only, never a reason to block or trim a package | The §14 budget 900,000 B, AR-B1 (+2,048 B), the `/autorig` gate 660,000 B, ST-19's 700,000 B, ST-B1 per-package shares; closes **OQ-3** |
| LIC-1 | **CC0-1.0 for game contract data and the pinned protocol schemas** (CC-Q3, GP-Q2 as defaulted by C0) | Any game or tool may copy `ekza.game-contract.v1` documents and schemas without terms | — |
| MERGE-1 | **Ready OMOBA work merges into `dev`** when it breaks nothing (green CI or local gate); `dev` is where the whole loop is tested before the RU and international releases | omoba-bevy PR #109 (contract exporter) and later contract PRs go to `dev` without waiting for a review round; `main`, releases and production stay the owner's | — |

## T. M7 G-a: the AI intake operators (2026-10-24)

The local, key-free clean-up of MODELING-PLAN §7.2 G-a, built on branch `m7/ga-intake`
([wp-notes/m7-ga.md](wp-notes/m7-ga.md)): `@ekza-studio/mesh-wasm` (meshoptimizer in a worker) and the operators
`mesh.normalize`, `mesh.reduce`, `mesh.paletteBake`, `mesh.textureDownsize` and the intake preset `mesh.intake`.

### T.1 Build decisions

| ID | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| M7-GA-1 | **meshoptimizer 1.3.0 pinned** (MIT), the registry's latest on 2026-10-24 (published 2026-09-25); only `meshoptimizer/simplifier` is imported, on first use, in the intake worker | The plan named 1.3.0; checked against the registry; the simplifier module embeds its WASM (≈ 20 kB gzip) |
| M7-GA-2 | **Wedges**: material slot, `paint` slot, `uv0`, `uv1` and `color0` split simplifier vertices (normals do not; they are recomputed at an 80° hard angle, M7-GA-17) | meshoptimizer infers seams from coincident positions and collapses a seam only along itself: no crack is possible |
| M7-GA-3 | **`vertex_lock` on UV seams is an option, off by default; on palette and material borders it is on** | Locking every seam point of a 241-chart atlas (6,593 of 48k points) forbids 3k triangles; seams are crack-free without it |
| M7-GA-4 | **Exact target by bisection** over the requested index count (≤ 10 runs); `reduce.target-unreached` when locks stop it | §7.2 "exact triangle target found by search"; a closed mesh loses triangles in pairs (odd targets end one under) |
| M7-GA-5 | **Pinch repair**: new edges with more than two faces lock their points' one-ring and the search runs again (≤ 2 rounds); `reduce.non-manifold` if any remain | meshoptimizer keeps attribute topology, not the welded mesh's link condition (seen where 220 UV charts meet) |
| M7-GA-6 | **Palette bake takes each face's dominant colour**: area-weighted samples, k-means in OKLab on a 1/256 histogram with deterministic seeding, the swatch a face's samples cover most | A mean turns a striped face into a colour the model does not have |
| M7-GA-7 | **A baked mesh is palette-coloured and paintable**: one new palette per run, `material {preset: 'unlit'}`, `source: null`, `colourOutput: 'palette-texture'`; the made-editable meshes of imported characters are refused | Imported meshes cannot be painted; the surgeon needs `source` on an asset character's meshes |
| M7-GA-8 | **Normalize bakes into the data** over the combined bounds of the targets (identity world placement, pivots reset, non-target children keep their world); skinned and character-owned meshes are refused | The parts of one model move together; after rigging a frame change is the skeleton's |
| M7-GA-9 | **The chain runs in a module worker** (`editor/src/workers/intake.worker.ts`), created on the first job, the same function inline without workers; one job at a time; a failed worker sends later jobs inline | The skin worker's pattern; the same bytes either way |
| M7-GA-10 | **Decimate first is `mesh.intake {bytes}`**: import and intake in one undo step, Make editable's 200k gate lifted for its own chain (≤ 4M triangles); `mesh.importStatic` and Auto-Rig keep their refusal | Q-MOD-K-11: no record above 200k ever exists; Auto-Rig's *Decimate first* button is its shell's (open) |
| M7-GA-11 | **PNG decoding is ours** (`io/png/decode.ts`); JPEG/WebP through `createImageBitmap` without colour conversion; unreadable textures fall back to the factor with a warning | The same pixels in Node and every browser; `sharp` stays banned |
| M7-GA-12 | **Texture downsize writes a new model asset** (PNG images, the BIN laid out again) and repoints the material slots | Asset records are immutable by hash |
| M7-GA-13 | **Quadrangulate is on in the intake preset** (30°, seams, hard edges and materials kept), an option there, automatic nowhere else | Q-MOD-K-9 |
| M7-GA-14 | **The acceptance input is procedural** (`io/testing` `makeAiLikeGlb`: 96,824 triangles, a 241-chart atlas on a black background with 2 texels of padding, facing +X); no binary fixture | No CC0 50k–200k textured GLB in the fixtures; deterministic and licence-clean |
| M7-GA-15 | **The palette bake never reads the atlas background**: samples inset 25 % toward each triangle's centroid, nearest texel, only texels inside the charts (rasterized from the UV triangles as imported) that are not transparent; a face with no valid sample takes its nearest valid neighbour's slot | Fix round 1 (review of `ga-after.png`): on a black gutter without padding the old bake gave 24 of 3,000 faces a darkened colour; now 0 |
| M7-GA-16 | **Clusters within OKLab 0.03 merge; unused clusters leave the palette** | Texture noise split each colour into two or three swatches; blend clusters were never chosen |
| M7-GA-17 | **Hard edges above 80° after a reduce** (was 60°) | 60° left ≈ 216 hard edges on a reduced character, drawn as dark lines along the limbs in Model; 80° keeps ≈ 48 and a box prop's 90° edges |

### T.2 Contract (the Halo Make stage, T8, and the Generate panel, G-d, build against it)

| ID | Contract | Note |
|---|---|---|
| M7-GA-CONTRACT-1 | `mesh.reduce { preset: 'prop' \| 'character' \| 'hero' = 'character', triangles: int \| null = null, …advanced, meshes? }` → `IntakeOutcome`; `triangles: null` = the preset's budget (Prop 500, Character 3,000, Hero 10,000), a number overrides it; one budget split over the targets | As T8 asked; advanced params (`uvWeight`, `normalWeight`, `lockSeams`, `lockPalette`, `lockBorder`, `regularize`, `prune`, `hardAngle`) all default |
| M7-GA-CONTRACT-2 | `mesh.normalize { height: number \| null = null, forward = '+z', up = '+y', unit = 'm', origin = 'bottom-center', meshes? }`: the result is always +Y up, +Z forward, metres, origin at the bottom centre; `forward`/`up` describe the **source** | As T8 asked; G-d passes `forward: '+x'` for Tripo |
| M7-GA-CONTRACT-3 | `mesh.paletteBake { colours: int = 16, samples = 6, meshes? }` | **`colours`, not `colors`**: the codebase spells colour British in identifiers (`colourOutput`, Shading `'colours'`); kept by the coordinator's rule, sent to the coordinator |
| M7-GA-CONTRACT-4 | `mesh.intake { preset = 'character', height: number \| null = null, colours = 16, triangles?, forward?, up?, quadrangulate = true, texture: 'palette' \| 'keep' = 'palette', textureSize = 1024, meshes?, bytes?, name? }`: ONE operator and ONE undo step (normalize → reduce → quadrangulate → palette bake); `height: null` = 1.70 m for characters and heroes, the model's own size for props; with `bytes` it imports in the same step and decimates meshes over 200k triangles first | As T8 asked, plus the import |
| M7-GA-CONTRACT-5 | Targets: `meshes` (mesh ids; group ids stand for their meshes), else the selected mesh objects — the mesh operators' convention; `mesh.textureDownsize { size = 1024, meshes? }` | The usual target rule |
| M7-GA-CONTRACT-6 | Every run returns `IntakeOutcome { meshes, palette, triangles {before, after}, colours, steps [{kind, ms, trianglesBefore, trianglesAfter, facts}], warnings, aiMarksMayBeAltered, worker, ms }`; polls answer plainly ("Select a mesh", "Already under the budget (N triangles)", "Normalize works before rigging: …"); surfaces: Mesh menu items, Halo More rows (Make) with plain labels, chips and ⚙ Options (param-lint), agent policy denied | `aiMarksMayBeAltered` is what ADR 0017 decision 7 records in the provenance (G-d writes it) |
